### Improving LLM-Based Fault Localization with External Memory and Project Context
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03585v1)
- **Authors**: Inseok Yeo, Duksan Ryu, Jongmoon Baik
- **Abstract**: Fault localization, the process of identifying the software components responsible for failures, is essential but often time-consuming. Recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) have enabled fault localization without extensive defect datasets or model fine-tuning. However, existing LLM-based methods rely only on general LLM capabilities and lack integration of project-specific knowledge, resulting in limited effectiveness, especially for complex software. We introduce MemFL, a novel approach that enhances LLM-based fault localization by integrating project-specific knowledge via external memory. This memory includes static summaries of the project and dynamic, iterative debugging insights gathered from previous attempts. By leveraging external memory, MemFL simplifies debugging into three streamlined steps, significantly improving efficiency and accuracy. Iterative refinement through dynamic memory further enhances reasoning quality over time. Evaluated on the Defects4J benchmark, MemFL using GPT-4o-mini localized 12.7% more bugs than current LLM-based methods, achieving this improvement with just 21% of the execution time (17.4 seconds per bug) and 33% of the API cost (0.0033 dollars per bug). On complex projects, MemFL's advantage increased to 27.6%. Additionally, MemFL with GPT-4.1-mini outperformed existing methods by 24.4%, requiring only 24.7 seconds and 0.0094 dollars per bug. MemFL thus demonstrates significant improvements by effectively incorporating project-specific knowledge into LLM-based fault localization, delivering high accuracy with reduced time and cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Improving LLM-Based Fault Localization with External Memory and Project Context":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MemFL, a novel fault localization technique that enhances Large Language Model (LLM)-based approaches by incorporating project-specific knowledge through an external memory system. The external memory consists of two parts: static memory (pre-generated summaries of the project and its classes) and dynamic memory (iteratively gathers debugging guidance from prior attempts). MemFL structures the debugging process into three steps: Bug Review Generation, Code Condensation, and Fault Confirmation. The authors demonstrate that MemFL, when using GPT-40-mini, achieves higher Top-1 accuracy compared to existing LLM-based baselines on the Defects4J benchmark, while also significantly reducing execution time and API cost. The performance gains are more pronounced on complex projects. The use of GPT-4.1-mini further improves the results. Ablation studies demonstrate the effectiveness of each component of MemFL.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the explicit incorporation of project-specific knowledge into an LLM-based fault localization approach via a structured external memory mechanism.  This addresses a crucial limitation of existing LLM-based methods, which often rely solely on the LLM's inherent knowledge and can struggle with complex projects requiring domain expertise. The dynamic memory component, which iteratively learns from previous debugging attempts, is a further novel contribution. The structured three-step approach also adds to the practical application of LLM.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from several aspects:

    *   **Improved accuracy:** The demonstrated improvement in fault localization accuracy, especially on complex projects like Closure, directly translates to reduced debugging time and cost for developers.
    *   **Reduced cost:**  The significant reduction in execution time and API costs compared to other LLM-based approaches makes the technique more practical and accessible for wider adoption. It mitigates a key barrier to entry for using LLMs in software engineering tasks.
    *   **Generalizability:**  While the evaluation is limited to Java projects on Defects4J, the underlying principle of incorporating project-specific knowledge is applicable to other programming languages and domains. The inclusion of GPT-4.1 also provides an aspect to the model's generalizability.
    *   **Insights:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of different components of MemFL, guiding future research and development in this area. The determination of an optimal dynamic memory generation policy is also a useful contribution.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:**  The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing LLM-based fault localization techniques.
    *   **Novel solution:** MemFL offers a compelling and well-structured solution.
    *   **Strong empirical evaluation:** The experiments on Defects4J provide convincing evidence of the effectiveness of MemFL. The overlap analysis is particularly insightful.
    *   **Thorough analysis:** The ablation studies and dynamic memory generation policy analysis provide valuable insights.
    *   **Clear and concise writing:**  The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited benchmark:** The evaluation is primarily limited to the Defects4J benchmark, which, while widely used, may not fully represent the diversity of real-world software projects. It will be interesting to see the result for projects not only from Defects4j dataset.
    *   **Java-centric:** The approach is evaluated only on Java projects, raising questions about its generalizability to other languages.
    *   **LLM reliance:** The performance of MemFL is inherently tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLM. Advancements in LLMs could impact the relative benefits of MemFL.
    *   **Dynamic memory generation:** While the paper presents an "optimal" dynamic memory generation policy, the process is still somewhat heuristic. A more rigorous and automated approach to generating and refining dynamic memory could be beneficial.
    *   **Static memory scalability:** For very large projects, pre-generating and managing static memory (project and class summaries) could become a challenge. The efficiency of the method can be improved by the static memory generation method.
    *   **Indirect dataset exposure:** Despite the authors’ efforts, the possibility that the LLM used in the experiment has seen the Defects4J benchmark still remains a threat.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence research on LLM-based software engineering tools. The external memory approach could be adapted and extended to other tasks, such as automated program repair, code summarization, and vulnerability detection. The findings about the importance of project-specific knowledge and the benefits of iterative refinement will likely guide future research in this area.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

MemFL represents a significant advancement in LLM-based fault localization by addressing a key limitation: the lack of project-specific knowledge. The external memory approach is novel, well-structured, and demonstrably effective. The paper is well-written and presents a strong empirical evaluation. While the evaluation is somewhat limited by the Java-centric focus and reliance on Defects4J, the potential impact of the work on the field is substantial. The approach of incorporating external knowledge has the potential to be applied in a variety of contexts, leading to a greater accuracy and efficiency. Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution to the field and warrants a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of scholarly literature makes it increasingly difficult for researchers to keep up with new knowledge. Automated tools are now more essential than ever to help navigate and interpret this vast body of information. ### Resolving Task Objective Conflicts in Unified Multimodal Understanding and Generation via Task-Aware Mixture-of-Experts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03591v1)
- **Authors**: Jiaxing Zhang, Xinyi Zeng, Hao Tang
- **Abstract**: Unified multimodal large language models (MLLMs) based on end-to-end autoregressive (AR) transformers effectively integrate both understanding and generation tasks within a single framework. However, intrinsic Task Objective Conflicts between high-level semantic abstraction in understanding and fine-grained detail preservation in generation pose significant challenges, often leading to suboptimal trade-offs and task interference. Existing solutions, such as decoupling shared visual encoders, fall short of fundamentally resolving these conflicts due to inherent AR architecture. In this paper, we propose a novel approach that decouples internal components of AR to resolve task objective conflicts. Specifically, we design UTAMoE, a Unified Task-Aware Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) framework that decouples internal AR modules via a Task-Aware MoE Layer to create task-specific optimization subpaths. To enhance task differentiation while maintaining overall coordination, we introduce a novel Two-Stage Training Strategy. Extensive experiments on multimodal benchmarks demonstrate that UTAMoE mitigates task objective conflicts, achieving state-of-the-art performance across various tasks. Visualizations and ablation studies further validate the effectiveness of our approach.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary**

The paper addresses the problem of task objective conflicts in unified multimodal large language models (MLLMs) that perform both understanding and generation tasks.  The core idea is that understanding requires high-level semantic abstraction, while generation needs fine-grained detail preservation, leading to suboptimal performance.  The authors propose UTAMoE, a Unified Task-Aware Mixture-of-Experts framework, which decouples internal AR modules using a Task-Aware MoE Layer.  This creates task-specific optimization subpaths within the AR architecture. A two-stage training strategy is used to further enhance task differentiation.  Experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across several multimodal benchmarks, suggesting that UTAMoE mitigates task objective conflicts.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the *internal decoupling* of an autoregressive architecture for multimodal tasks using a Task-Aware MoE layer. While MoEs have been explored in LLMs, applying them in this specific way, within the AR architecture and tailored for understanding/generation conflict resolution, appears novel.  The two-stage training strategy is also a contribution.  However, it's important to carefully consider the extent to which this builds upon prior work in MoE routing and task-specific experts. The Janus method already attempts encoder-level task-specific decoupling, making it crucial to assess how UTAMoE fundamentally transcends its limitations.

*   **Significance:** Resolving task objective conflicts is a critical challenge in unified multimodal models. If UTAMoE genuinely achieves this, it could significantly improve the performance of MLLMs on diverse tasks. The reported improvements in both understanding and generation are promising. The paper's significance hinges on whether UTAMoE offers a practical and scalable solution that is demonstrably superior to existing approaches, especially in complex, real-world scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of task objective conflicts and its impact on unified MLLMs.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** UTAMoE is a well-defined framework with a clear architecture and training strategy.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The reported state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmarks is a significant strength.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:**  The provided qualitative results (image comparisons, expert utilization) offer insights into how UTAMoE mitigates task objective conflicts.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** These help to isolate the impact of the key components (Task-Aware MoE, training strategy).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Contribution:** The degree to which UTAMoE goes beyond existing MoE and task-specific routing methods needs further clarification. While it innovates in its application within the AR, MLLM architecture, the MoE component itself should be justified in how it avoids shortcomings of existing solutions.
    *   **Limited Scope of Qualitative Analysis:** While the visual results are presented, a more in-depth qualitative analysis with more intricate examples demonstrating concrete improvements would be beneficial.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** How stable the approach is across different tasks and datasets. Do parameters need to be tuned for each individual task?  Sensitivity to hyperparameter tuning is a practical limitation that should be addressed.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Are there any disadvantages in terms of computational resource demands?

*   **Impact and Influence:** If the results are reproducible and hold up under scrutiny, UTAMoE could influence the design of future unified multimodal models. It could lead to more effective ways of decoupling internal model components to handle conflicting task objectives. The impact will depend on its generalizability, scalability, and practical applicability.

*   **Justification for Score:**  The paper addresses an important problem in a growing field and presents a technically sound approach with compelling experimental results. However, it builds upon existing work, and the quantitative jump in performance might not always justify the complexity.

### ControlThinker: Unveiling Latent Semantics for Controllable Image Generation through Visual Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03596v1)
- **Authors**: Feng Han, Yang Jiao, Shaoxiang Chen, Junhao Xu, Jingjing Chen, Yu-Gang Jiang
- **Abstract**: The field of controllable image generation has seen significant advancements, with various architectures improving generation layout consistency with control signals. However, contemporary methods still face challenges in bridging the semantic gap between input text prompts with sparse semantics and the target images, often over-relying on low-level control signals to infer regional details. To address this challenge, we propose ControlThinker, a novel framework that employs a "comprehend-then-generate" paradigm. Firstly, by incentivizing the visual reasoning capability of a MLLM, latent semantics from control images are mined to enrich text prompts. This enriched semantic understanding then seamlessly aids in image generation without the need for additional complex modifications. To further tackle the uncertainty arising from the ambiguity of control images, we encourage broader exploration of reasoning trajectories and select the optimal one using a metric-based output reward model (ORM). Extensive experimental results demonstrate that ControlThinker effectively mitigates the semantic gap between raw text prompts and target images, resulting in improved visual quality and semantic consistency across a wide range of benchmarks. The code and models are available at https://github.com/Maplebb/ControlThinker.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the ControlThinker paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ControlThinker, a novel framework for controllable image generation. It addresses the semantic gap between sparse text prompts and detailed target images by employing a "comprehend-then-generate" paradigm. First, a fine-tuned Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) analyzes control images to extract latent semantics, effectively enriching the text prompt.  This enriched prompt then guides the image generation process. To handle uncertainties in the control images, the framework explores multiple reasoning trajectories and selects the optimal one using a metric-based output reward model (ORM).  Experiments demonstrate improved visual quality and semantic consistency across various benchmarks without modifying the underlying image generators.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using an MLLM to enrich text prompts for controllable image generation is relatively novel. Prior work has focused on modifying the image generation architecture or directly feeding control signals. Leveraging the reasoning capabilities of MLLMs to extract higher-level semantic information from control images and incorporate this information into the text prompt is a distinct contribution. The two-stage training process (SFT and RFT) for the MLLM, tailored to handle control images, is also a novel aspect. The use of a metric-based ORM to select the best reasoning trajectory further adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical limitation of current controllable image generation methods: the reliance on low-level control signals and the inability to bridge the semantic gap between text and image. ControlThinker's approach significantly improves semantic consistency and visual quality, indicating a substantial step forward.  The results demonstrate clear improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods across multiple control types. Furthermore, the modular design allows for potential integration with various existing image generators.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined framework with clear explanations of each component (MLLM, SFT/RFT training, ORM).
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple benchmarks and control types.
    *   Ablation studies that demonstrate the effectiveness of each component.
    *   Qualitative results show significant improvements in semantic coherence and visual quality.
    *   Provides a viable solution to address the limitations of relying on low-level image control.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on a curated dataset for fine-tuning the MLLM could be a limitation. The size and quality of this dataset are crucial for the performance of the framework. However, the paper has shown that the curated dataset has better quality and the authors are generous with details of how it was generated.
    *   The improvement could also potentially vary significantly depending on the quality of the control image.
    *   The paper acknowledges this uncertainty, they have introduced the Inference Scaling to mitigate.
    *   While the paper shows improvements in both semantic consistency and visual quality, it does not provide a comprehensive analysis on the limitations and failures of the system.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   ControlThinker has the potential to influence future research in controllable image generation by shifting the focus towards leveraging higher-level semantic understanding.
    *   The framework could be extended to other related tasks, such as image editing and video generation.
    *   The modular design of ControlThinker could encourage the development of new reasoning modules and reward models.

* **Justification of Score:**
While ControlThinker presents a novel approach and demonstrates significant improvements in controllable image generation, the dependency on a curated dataset for fine-tuning the MLLM, the potential for performance variation based on the control image, and the limited analysis on the potential limitations affect its overall score. However, the significant improvements shown over state-of-the-art methods, clear problem definition, and the potential influence on the field do merit a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Auto prompt sql: a resource-efficient architecture for text-to-sql translation in constrained environments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03598v1)
- **Authors**: Zetong Tang, Qian Ma, Di Wu
- **Abstract**: Using the best Text-to-SQL methods in resource-constrained environments is challenging due to their reliance on resource-intensive open-source models. This paper introduces Auto Prompt SQL(AP-SQL), a novel architecture designed to bridge the gap between resource-efficient small open-source models and the powerful capabilities of large closed-source models for Text-to-SQL translation. Our method decomposes the task into schema filtering, retrieval-augmented text-to-SQL generation based on in-context examples, and prompt-driven schema linking and SQL generation. To improve schema selection accuracy, we fine-tune large language models. Crucially, we also explore the impact of prompt engineering throughout the process, leveraging Chain-of-Thought(CoT) and Graph-of-Thought(GoT) templates to significantly enhance the model's reasoning for accurate SQL generation. Comprehensive evaluations on the Spider benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of AP-SQL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Auto Prompt SQL (AP-SQL), a novel architecture designed to improve Text-to-SQL performance in resource-constrained environments. AP-SQL leverages a modular approach, combining schema filtering (using a fine-tuned Qwen-3B model), retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) based on in-context examples, and prompt engineering techniques (Chain-of-Thought and Graph-of-Thought) to generate SQL queries. The approach aims to bridge the gap between the capabilities of smaller, open-source models and larger, closed-source models in Text-to-SQL translation. The authors conduct experiments on the Spider benchmark, demonstrating improvements over existing methods across various large language models (LLMs).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The novelty lies in the combination of several techniques into a coherent architecture tailored for resource-constrained scenarios. The schema filtering component, leveraging a fine-tuned small language model, is a good idea. Using RAG to select in-context examples is a fairly standard approach. The application of both Chain-of-Thought (CoT) and Graph-of-Thought (GoT) prompts, based on query complexity, is a reasonable strategy, but not fundamentally new. The combination of these elements, designed to make smaller open-source models more useful, demonstrates a focus on a pragmatic and relevant problem. However, each component individually is fairly standard, with novelty mostly stemming from the integration and tailoring to specific resource constraints.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work rests on its potential to make Text-to-SQL more accessible in environments where large, closed-source models are not feasible due to cost or infrastructure limitations. The experiments on the Spider dataset show performance improvements compared to other methods.  The performance improvements, particularly when leveraging the components individually to assess the impact of each technique, would have strengthened the analysis and clarified where the architectural contributions are realized.  The focus on resource efficiency is a positive aspect, reflecting the real-world constraints many practitioners face. The paper provides empirical support for the effectiveness of the architecture. A more thorough analysis of the computational cost versus the gain in performance would be ideal.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a practical and important problem: making Text-to-SQL viable in resource-constrained settings.
    *   The modular design allows for potential component reuse and adaptation.
    *   Uses a well-established benchmark (Spider) for evaluation.
    *   Demonstrates solid performance improvements.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Individual components are not particularly novel. The innovation stems from the integration.
    *   Limited ablation studies to assess the contributions of each component within the architecture. Need a comprehensive analysis regarding relative gain from CoT and GoT.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of computational cost versus performance gain.
    *   The description and justification for the specific schema linking approach could be more detailed.

*   **Potential Influence:** AP-SQL has the potential to influence the direction of Text-to-SQL research, encouraging the development of more resource-efficient solutions. It is replicable and could be used as a baseline for future work. Also, the integration may inspire the research community in adopting and improving this paradigm.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a useful and practical architecture for Text-to-SQL, achieving performance improvements on a standard benchmark. The primary strength lies in the integration of existing techniques to address a relevant problem (resource efficiency). However, the lack of significant novelty in individual components and the relatively limited ablation studies restrain the overall score.  A more detailed analysis of the trade-offs between performance and computational cost and a deeper dive into the schema linking strategy would strengthen the work and justify a higher rating. The potential for impact exists but hinges on follow-up research that builds upon these foundations and thoroughly evaluates the individual contribution of each component.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Learning to Insert [PAUSE] Tokens for Better Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03616v1)
- **Authors**: Eunki Kim, Sangryul Kim, James Thorne
- **Abstract**: To enhance reasoning capabilities, previous works have explored incorporating special-purpose tokens into the training process. These strategies strengthen the learning mechanism of transformer-based large language models (LLMs). Building on prior research, in which inserting dummy tokens consecutively just before reasoning steps can enhance effectiveness, we introduce a novel approach termed Dynamic Inserting Tokens Training (DIT). Our method identifies positions within sequences where model confidence is lowest according to token log-likelihood. Strategically inserting [PAUSE] tokens on these positions bolsters the model's predictive capabilities for subsequent tokens. Experimental results across diverse datasets and models, from the 2.7B model to the 8B model, demonstrate that DIT consistently outperforms traditional fine-tuning and previous token insertion methods. With this simple yet effective method, we achieve accuracy gains of up to 4.7%p on GSM8K, 3.23%p on AQUA-RAT, and pass@1 improvements of up to 3.4%p on MBPP datasets. Our work shows a model-based, dynamic approach rather than a heuristic one, thereby broadening the scope of research in reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Dynamic Inserting Tokens Training" (DIT), a novel method to improve reasoning capabilities in large language models (LLMs). DIT strategically inserts "[PAUSE]" tokens at positions in a sequence where the model exhibits low confidence, as measured by token log-likelihood. The authors hypothesize that this insertion enhances the model's ability to predict subsequent tokens, leading to improved performance on reasoning tasks. They demonstrate the effectiveness of DIT across various datasets (GSM8K, AQUA-RAT, MBPP) and models (Phi-2, Phi-3 Mini, Llama 3), showing consistent improvements over traditional fine-tuning and existing token insertion techniques.  The paper also analyzes the impact of [PAUSE] tokens on training dynamics, token probability distributions, and model robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamically inserting tokens based on token log-likelihood is the paper's main contribution. While token insertion itself isn't entirely new (Goyal et al., 2024), the dynamic selection based on model confidence is a step forward.  The comparison to focal loss and noise-based regularization methods helps contextualize the approach, but also highlights that the underlying principles are not entirely revolutionary. The degree of automation and model-based rather than heuristic-based insertion is another point of difference, broadening the scope beyond the task-specific adaptations. However, there appears to be little adaptation of training beyond the specific positions for insertion during DIT, suggesting that the paper could have been more ambitious with exploring other parameters to improve model reasoning skills.
*   **Significance:** The results are significant in that they show consistent performance gains across several models and datasets. The improvements on GSM8K and AQUA-RAT are particularly noteworthy. This suggests that DIT is a generally applicable technique for enhancing reasoning abilities in LLMs. The analysis of token probability distributions and loss calibration provides insights into *why* the method works, strengthening the contribution. The code release is a positive step, facilitating reproducibility and further research. The paper highlights the need to refine and optimize deployment and deployment of tokens, and acknowledges that the black box interpretations need further comprehension.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-written paper with a clearly articulated problem and proposed solution.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets and model architectures.
    *   In-depth analysis of the impact of [PAUSE] tokens on model behavior.
    *   Release of code for reproducibility.
    *   Acknowledges limitations and potential unintended consequences.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The underlying intuition, while reasonable, isn't entirely groundbreaking. Token insertion methods are not novel in and of themselves.
    *   While dynamic, the method still relies on a relatively simple insertion strategy. More sophisticated methods for placing tokens, considering broader context, could potentially yield greater gains.
    *   The ablation studies, while useful, could be more extensive. For instance, exploring different log-likelihood thresholds for token insertion might provide further insights.
    *   The paper offers no theoretical analysis or guarantees for the performance of DIT.

**Justification of Score:**

Despite the weaknesses, DIT offers a valuable contribution to the field of LLM reasoning. The method's simplicity and consistent performance gains make it a practical and readily implementable technique. The analysis of the training dynamics and token probability distributions is a strong point. It shows evidence of how the method functions, suggesting more robust model training. This is also a method for automating the position for tokens.

The overall novelty is fairly moderate, as is the theoretical underpinning. I would give the paper a score of **6**.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Do Large Language Models Know Folktales? A Case Study of Yokai in Japanese Folktales
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03619v1)
- **Authors**: Ayuto Tsutsumi, Yuu Jinnai
- **Abstract**: Although Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong language understanding and generation abilities across various languages, their cultural knowledge is often limited to English-speaking communities, which can marginalize the cultures of non-English communities. To address the problem, evaluation of the cultural awareness of the LLMs and the methods to develop culturally aware LLMs have been investigated. In this study, we focus on evaluating knowledge of folktales, a key medium for conveying and circulating culture. In particular, we focus on Japanese folktales, specifically on knowledge of Yokai. Yokai are supernatural creatures originating from Japanese folktales that continue to be popular motifs in art and entertainment today. Yokai have long served as a medium for cultural expression, making them an ideal subject for assessing the cultural awareness of LLMs. We introduce YokaiEval, a benchmark dataset consisting of 809 multiple-choice questions (each with four options) designed to probe knowledge about yokai. We evaluate the performance of 31 Japanese and multilingual LLMs on this dataset. The results show that models trained with Japanese language resources achieve higher accuracy than English-centric models, with those that underwent continued pretraining in Japanese, particularly those based on Llama-3, performing especially well. The code and dataset are available at https://github.com/CyberAgentA ILab/YokaiEval.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces YokaiEval, a benchmark dataset designed to assess Large Language Models' (LLMs) knowledge of Yokai from Japanese folktales. The authors create YokaiEval by extracting question-answer pairs from Wikipedia articles, using GPT-4o for generation and GPT-4omini and manual verification for curation. They evaluate 31 LLMs, including Japanese-centric, English-centric, and Chinese-centric models, and find that Japanese-centric models perform better, especially those continually pre-trained on Japanese data. The paper investigates the impact of pre-training, supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and preference learning (PL) on acquiring cultural knowledge, suggesting continuous pre-training is key. The paper also analyzes the dataset and potential data leakage issues.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel benchmark dataset, YokaiEval, specifically targeted at assessing cultural knowledge within LLMs. While cultural awareness evaluation is not entirely new, focusing on folktales and, specifically, Yokai is a unique angle. The detailed analysis of the dataset creation and evaluation methodologies also adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The findings have important implications for developing culturally aware LLMs. By demonstrating the superior performance of models trained with Japanese language resources, the study highlights the importance of language-specific training data in capturing cultural nuances. The investigation into pre-training, SFT, and PL contributes to our understanding of how to best instill cultural knowledge into LLMs.  The analysis of potential data leakage is also significant and highlights potential pitfalls. The dataset also serves as an important resource for future researchers working in this area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper's focus on evaluating LLMs' knowledge of folktales is compelling.
    *   The rigorous dataset creation process, including multiple stages of filtering and manual verification, enhances the reliability of YokaiEval.
    *   The comprehensive evaluation of 31 LLMs provides valuable insights into their cultural awareness.
    *   The investigation into the impact of different training procedures (pre-training, SFT, PL) sheds light on effective methods for developing culturally aware LLMs.
    *   The dataset and code availability are strengths.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on Wikipedia as the primary source of information may introduce bias, as it represents a specific viewpoint. Although the authors manually curated the data to mitigate this limitation, there is still a potential for some bias.
    *   The analysis of data leakage is limited. While the paper presents an analysis of data leakage, a more thorough investigation, such as using methods like extracting known memorized data from models, may yield more insights.
    *   The generalizability of the findings to other cultures and folktale domains may be limited, as the study focuses exclusively on Yokai.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on cultural awareness in LLMs by providing a benchmark dataset, evaluation methodology, and insights into effective training procedures. The study could encourage the development of more culturally sensitive LLMs that better represent the diversity of human knowledge and values.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the work is valuable, some elements like the analysis of model outputs or use of closed models limit the reproducibility and scope of the work. The single culture focus is also a weakness as the claims are specific to that culture.

Score: 7

**Rationale:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of cultural awareness in LLMs through the introduction of YokaiEval and its comprehensive evaluation of LLMs. The findings provide important insights for developing culturally sensitive LLMs. However, the reliance on Wikipedia, limited data leakage analysis, and narrow focus on Yokai limit the paper's overall impact. Further research is needed to validate the findings in other cultural contexts and develop more robust methodologies for evaluating and training culturally aware LLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Robustness of Prompting: Enhancing Robustness of Large Language Models Against Prompting Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03627v1)
- **Authors**: Lin Mu, Guowei Chu, Li Ni, Lei Sang, Zhize Wu, Peiquan Jin, Yiwen Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable performance across various tasks by effectively utilizing a prompting strategy. However, they are highly sensitive to input perturbations, such as typographical errors or slight character order errors, which can substantially degrade their performance. Despite advances in prompting techniques, developing a prompting strategy that explicitly mitigates the negative impact of such perturbations remains an open challenge. To bridge this gap, we propose Robustness of Prompting (RoP), a novel prompting strategy specifically designed to enhance the robustness of LLMs. RoP consists of two stages: Error Correction and Guidance. In the Error Correction stage, RoP applies diverse perturbation methods to generate adversarial examples, which are then used to construct prompts that automatically correct input errors. In the Guidance stage, RoP generates an optimal guidance prompting based on the corrected input, steering the model toward more robust and accurate inferences. Through comprehensive experiments spanning arithmetic, commonsense, and logical reasoning tasks, we demonstrate that RoP significantly improves LLMs' robustness against adversarial perturbations. Notably, it maintains model accuracy with only minimal degradation compared to clean input scenarios, thereby establishing RoP as a practical and effective approach for enhancing LLM robustness in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Robustness of Prompting" (RoP), a novel two-stage prompting strategy designed to enhance the robustness of Large Language Models (LLMs) against input perturbations (e.g., typos, character order errors). RoP consists of: (1) **Error Correction:** Generating adversarial examples by perturbing inputs and creating prompts that teach the LLM to restore the original intent.  (2) **Guidance:** Automatically generating optimal guidance prompts based on the corrected input to steer the model towards more accurate inferences.  The approach is evaluated across arithmetic, commonsense, and logical reasoning tasks, demonstrating improved robustness compared to standard prompting and other prompting techniques like Chain-of-Thought, APE, and PromptAgent. The authors also include ablation studies and analyze robustness across different perturbation levels and model architectures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper proposes a novel prompting strategy that directly addresses the sensitivity of LLMs to input perturbations. While the idea of using adversarial examples for robustness is not entirely new, the specific application within a prompting framework, combined with the two-stage approach (error correction followed by optimized guidance), appears to be a significant step forward. The automatic generation of prompts to address these perturbations also enhances novelty. Existing methods either focus on improving prompting techniques without explicitly addressing robustness, or focus on adversarial attacks. RoP specifically tries to improve robustness *through* prompting.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical and well-known problem with LLMs: their fragility when faced with noisy or adversarial inputs.  Enhancing robustness has significant real-world implications for deploying LLMs in applications where input quality cannot be guaranteed. The experimental results demonstrate clear improvements in robustness across multiple datasets and perturbation types, making a compelling case for the effectiveness of RoP. Ablation studies give insight in to the importance of both stages of the RoP pipeline. Testing RoP against different levels of noise also adds to the significance of the approach.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The RoP method consistently improves the accuracy under various attack settings across various settings.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation. The paper thoroughly evaluates RoP on multiple datasets, perturbation types, and even across different LLM architectures. This extensive evaluation strengthens the validity of the claims.
    *   Ablation studies are included demonstrating that both stages of the pipeline are important for effectiveness.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the RoP framework and the experimental setup.
    *   The performance against different levels of noise give insight into the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The perturbation types are limited to a set of five predefined categories. While these cover some common errors, the paper acknowledges that they do not encompass the full range of real-world noise (e.g., grammatical errors, pragmatic shifts). The reliance on synthetic perturbations might also limit generalizability.
    *   The method relies on GPT-4 to generate the instructions, meaning the LLM needs to be fairly capable to be able to benefit from RoP. This also may introduce some bias towards solutions that the GPT-4 thinks are valid.
    *   The paper lacks a deeper analysis of *why* RoP works. Understanding the specific mechanisms by which error correction and optimized guidance enhance robustness would be valuable.
    *   A comparison to more recent state-of-the-art robustness methods would strengthen the impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in prompting strategies, adversarial robustness for LLMs, and practical deployment of LLMs in noisy environments.  The RoP framework provides a solid foundation for future research aimed at developing more robust and reliable prompting techniques.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by addressing a key limitation of LLMs and proposing a practical and effective solution. While there are some limitations, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a significant and novel improvement in LLM robustness through a well-defined prompting strategy. The comprehensive evaluation and insightful ablation studies make a strong case for the RoP framework. While the reliance on synthetic perturbations and the limited analysis of the underlying mechanisms are weaknesses, the overall impact and potential influence of the paper justify a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### EmoArt: A Multidimensional Dataset for Emotion-Aware Artistic Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03652v1)
- **Authors**: Cheng Zhang, Hongxia xie, Bin Wen, Songhan Zuo, Ruoxuan Zhang, Wen-huang Cheng
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancement of diffusion models, text-to-image generation has achieved significant progress in image resolution, detail fidelity, and semantic alignment, particularly with models like Stable Diffusion 3.5, Stable Diffusion XL, and FLUX 1. However, generating emotionally expressive and abstract artistic images remains a major challenge, largely due to the lack of large-scale, fine-grained emotional datasets. To address this gap, we present the EmoArt Dataset -- one of the most comprehensive emotion-annotated art datasets to date. It contains 132,664 artworks across 56 painting styles (e.g., Impressionism, Expressionism, Abstract Art), offering rich stylistic and cultural diversity. Each image includes structured annotations: objective scene descriptions, five key visual attributes (brushwork, composition, color, line, light), binary arousal-valence labels, twelve emotion categories, and potential art therapy effects. Using EmoArt, we systematically evaluate popular text-to-image diffusion models for their ability to generate emotionally aligned images from text. Our work provides essential data and benchmarks for emotion-driven image synthesis and aims to advance fields such as affective computing, multimodal learning, and computational art, enabling applications in art therapy and creative design. The dataset and more details can be accessed via our project website.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the EmoArt paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EmoArt, a large-scale (132,664 artworks) dataset for emotion-aware artistic image analysis and generation.  It covers 56 painting styles and includes structured annotations for each image, including objective scene descriptions, five visual attributes (brushwork, composition, color, line, light), binary arousal-valence labels, twelve emotion categories, and potential art therapy effects. The authors use GPT-4o assisted annotation with human validation. The paper benchmarks state-of-the-art diffusion models for emotion alignment and visual coherence, highlighting the dataset's utility in affective computing, computational art, and well-being applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the **scale and richness** of the dataset specifically tailored for emotional analysis of *artistic* images. While prior datasets exist in the realm of emotion recognition, they typically focus on real-world photos and lack the detailed visual attribute annotations and art-specific categories. Combining these with Art Therapy potential is relatively novel. The use of a modern LLM (GPT-4o) for annotation is appropriate and yields a comprehensive annotation structure for each image. The benchmarking experiments validate its suitability for AIGC research and further emphasize the value of the annotations.

*   **Significance:** The EmoArt dataset addresses a significant gap in the field.  Generating emotionally expressive artistic images is a challenging task, and the lack of large-scale, well-annotated datasets has been a major impediment. EmoArt provides the research community with a valuable resource to develop and evaluate emotion-aware image generation models.  The inclusion of art therapy effects opens up new avenues for exploration. The comprehensive annotation structure allows for various research directions, including multimodal emotion grounding, style transfer, and the study of the interplay between visual attributes and emotional expression. It provides valuable insights to build better generation models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Large Scale and Diversity:** The dataset's size and stylistic diversity are a major strength, enabling robust training and evaluation of machine learning models.
    *   **Comprehensive Annotations:** The detailed and structured annotations are a significant asset, providing rich information for various tasks.
    *   **High Annotation Quality:** The GPT-4o assisted annotation, followed by human validation, ensures a high level of annotation quality.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough evaluation of state-of-the-art diffusion models using both quantitative and qualitative metrics.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:**  Explicit mention of using publicly accessible sources and rigorous filtering for content safety and image quality demonstrate that the authors have considered ethical use.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Potential Bias in Annotations:** While human validation is present, potential biases inherent in GPT-4o's training data or the annotators themselves could influence the emotional labels. More detailed analysis of annotation biases would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Subjectivity of Emotional Labels:** Emotional perception is inherently subjective.  While the paper acknowledges this through human validation, a more in-depth discussion of the challenges of capturing subjective emotional responses in art would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Art Therapy Effects:** The paper introduces the concept of art therapy potential but doesn't explore this aspect in detail within the evaluation. Future work could focus on this dimension.
*   **Impact and Influence:** The EmoArt dataset is likely to have a significant impact on the fields of affective computing, computational art, and multimodal learning. It provides a valuable resource for researchers to advance the development of emotion-aware image generation models and explore the therapeutic potential of art. The benchmarks provided will also serve as a useful reference point for future research.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field. The EmoArt dataset fills a crucial gap by providing a large-scale, well-annotated resource specifically for emotion-aware artistic image analysis and generation. The paper demonstrates that emotion-aware models can be trained more effectively on art datasets.  The inclusion of art therapy insights is a noteworthy addition. While there is subjectivity in emotional annotations and potential LLM bias, the authors have addressed these issues through a rigorous validation pipeline and discussion of annotation complexities. This, combined with solid benchmarking experiments makes for a strong contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### MambaNeXt-YOLO: A Hybrid State Space Model for Real-time Object Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03654v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaochun Lei, Siqi Wu, Weilin Wu, Zetao Jiang
- **Abstract**: Real-time object detection is a fundamental but challenging task in computer vision, particularly when computational resources are limited. Although YOLO-series models have set strong benchmarks by balancing speed and accuracy, the increasing need for richer global context modeling has led to the use of Transformer-based architectures. Nevertheless, Transformers have high computational complexity because of their self-attention mechanism, which limits their practicality for real-time and edge deployments. To overcome these challenges, recent developments in linear state space models, such as Mamba, provide a promising alternative by enabling efficient sequence modeling with linear complexity. Building on this insight, we propose MambaNeXt-YOLO, a novel object detection framework that balances accuracy and efficiency through three key contributions: (1) MambaNeXt Block: a hybrid design that integrates CNNs with Mamba to effectively capture both local features and long-range dependencies; (2) Multi-branch Asymmetric Fusion Pyramid Network (MAFPN): an enhanced feature pyramid architecture that improves multi-scale object detection across various object sizes; and (3) Edge-focused Efficiency: our method achieved 66.6\% mAP at 31.9 FPS on the PASCAL VOC dataset without any pre-training and supports deployment on edge devices such as the NVIDIA Jetson Xavier NX and Orin NX.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MambaNeXt-YOLO: A Hybrid State Space Model for Real-time Object Detection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MambaNeXt-YOLO, a novel object detection framework designed for real-time performance, particularly on resource-constrained edge devices. The core idea is to combine the strengths of Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) for local feature extraction with the efficiency of state space models (SSMs), specifically Mamba, for long-range dependency modeling.  The framework incorporates three key contributions: a MambaNeXt Block (a hybrid CNN-Mamba module), a Multi-branch Asymmetric Fusion Pyramid Network (MAFPN) for multi-scale feature fusion, and an emphasis on edge-focused efficiency.  The authors demonstrate that MambaNeXt-YOLO achieves a competitive mAP and FPS on the PASCAL VOC dataset, and can be deployed on NVIDIA Jetson devices.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the integration of the Mamba SSM into the YOLO object detection framework, alongside specifically engineered modules to leverage both CNN and SSM capabilities. While MambaYOLO already explores this combination, MambaNeXt-YOLO improves upon it with its hybrid block design and more sophisticated feature fusion strategy. The MAFPN is adapted from previous work (MHAF-YOLO) but is reimplemented with MambaNeXt modules, while the core CNN and ResGate block are not, in and of themselves, novel.

*   **Significance:** Real-time object detection on edge devices is a crucial area, and exploring alternatives to computationally expensive self-attention mechanisms (like those in Transformers) is a valid and important pursuit.  Mamba presents a promising alternative due to its linear complexity. Demonstrating that Mamba can be effectively integrated into a YOLO-based detector, and that it can achieve competitive performance in terms of both accuracy and speed, is a significant contribution to the field. Furthermore, the specific optimizations and design choices targeting edge deployment are valuable practical contributions. The ablation studies provide useful insights into the contribution of each component of the design.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The hybrid CNN-Mamba architecture is a well-motivated design decision, aiming to address the weaknesses of each approach while leveraging their strengths.
    *   The MAFPN enhances multi-scale feature representation, which is crucial for object detection tasks.
    *   The edge-focused efficiency considerations are practical and make the work applicable to real-world deployment scenarios.
    *   The ablation studies provide thorough support for design choices, including integration of the ConvNext and ResGate.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the MambaNeXt block is a novel integration, individual components are less innovative, making the overall system incremental rather than fundamentally groundbreaking.
    *   Although the MAFPN improves object detection across scales, its core structure is adopted from the MAFPN in MHAF-YOLO, limiting its innovative edge.
    *   The performance gain compared to other YOLO variants is relatively modest, especially considering the increase in model complexity (e.g. parameters, FLOPS).
    *   While the paper mentions DOTA v1.5, it should be noted that the performance metric used for comparison is not typical of this dataset, and more recent, more robust models have demonstrated higher performance.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by demonstrating the feasibility of using Mamba-based SSMs in object detection. It also provides a useful set of design principles and architectural components that can be adopted and adapted by other researchers working on real-time object detection for edge devices.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a worthwhile extension to the field of real-time object detection by successfully integrating Mamba SSMs into the YOLO framework. However, the novelty of the core components (CNN and ResGate) is somewhat limited, and the performance gains while present, are not transformative relative to the increase in model complexity. The contribution is valuable for its practical edge-focused optimizations and demonstration of Mamba in a real-world application, but the work is more incremental than revolutionary. The MAFPN, while effective, borrows heavily from past work, and the DOTA metrics are not standard.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Facts are Harder Than Opinions -- A Multilingual, Comparative Analysis of LLM-Based Fact-Checking Reliability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03655v1)
- **Authors**: Lorraine Saju, Arnim Bleier, Jana Lasser, Claudia Wagner
- **Abstract**: The proliferation of misinformation necessitates scalable, automated fact-checking solutions. Yet, current benchmarks often overlook multilingual and topical diversity. This paper introduces a novel, dynamically extensible data set that includes 61,514 claims in multiple languages and topics, extending existing datasets up to 2024. Through a comprehensive evaluation of five prominent Large Language Models (LLMs), including GPT-4o, GPT-3.5 Turbo, LLaMA 3.1, and Mixtral 8x7B, we identify significant performance gaps between different languages and topics. While overall GPT-4o achieves the highest accuracy, it declines to classify 43% of claims. Across all models, factual-sounding claims are misclassified more often than opinions, revealing a key vulnerability. These findings underscore the need for caution and highlight challenges in deploying LLM-based fact-checking systems at scale.
- **Summary**: This paper presents a novel, dynamically extensible, multilingual, and multi-topic dataset (FactSpan) containing 61,514 fact-checked claims across 30 languages, spanning 2007-2024. The authors use this dataset to comparatively analyze the fact-checking reliability of five prominent Large Language Models (LLMs): GPT-3.5 Turbo, GPT-40, LLaMA 3.1 (8B and 70B), and Mixtral 8x7B. The study investigates how claim characteristics (language, topic, type - factual vs. opinion, structural features) influence LLM fact-checking accuracy. The key findings include significant performance variations across languages and topics, a high refusal rate for GPT-40, and a systematic bias where factual statements are misclassified more often than opinions. The authors underscore the need for caution in deploying LLM-based fact-checking systems and highlight vulnerabilities in diverse real-world contexts. They make their dataset and code publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes several valuable contributions, but also exhibits some limitations.

*   **Novelty of the Dataset:** The creation of a large, dynamically extensible multilingual and multi-topic dataset is a significant contribution. Existing fact-checking datasets are often limited in language and topical coverage, making FactSpan a useful resource for future research. The dynamic extensibility ensures its continued relevance.

*   **Comparative Analysis:** The systematic comparison of multiple LLMs across a diverse dataset is insightful. The paper identifies specific weaknesses in LLM fact-checking, particularly the bias against factual statements and variations across languages.

*   **Focus on Claim Characteristics:** Analyzing how claim features influence LLM accuracy is a strength. Identifying that factual claims are harder to verify than opinions is a critical finding, highlighting that LLMs are potentially relying more on the structure of claims and less so factual verification. The impact of language adds to the nuance of the findings.

*   **Limitations:** The study's reliance on automated annotation for some claim features (topic, claim type, presence of numerical claims) introduces a potential source of error, despite manual validation on a small sample. It might have benefitted by including multiple independent human evaluators on the dataset creation. Furthermore, while the dataset is multilingual, some languages are underrepresented, potentially skewing the language-specific performance analysis. The claim that the dataset's design enables for easy updates is not rigorously demonstrated; more information would need to be offered, which currently appears to be omitted from both the paper and supplementary materials. The decision to simplify verdict labels to a binary true/false may have missed some nuances of how the models approach fact-checking, although they acknowledge this limitation and state they did so to be able to complete the evaluation. Finally, there is no detailed error analysis of incorrect LLM answers which would allow for a more nuanced review of the outcomes.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have significant implications for the deployment of LLM-based fact-checking systems. The highlighted vulnerabilities, especially the bias against factual statements, warrant caution and suggest the need for targeted safeguards. The insights into how claim characteristics influence LLM reliability can inform the development of more robust and reliable fact-checking solutions. The paper is relevant for researchers in natural language processing, computational social science, and information science, as well as practitioners in the fact-checking industry.

The strength in its novelty of dataset creation, as well as findings suggesting weaknesses in LLMs is enough to warrant a positive score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 7/10

### Trustworthy Medical Question Answering: An Evaluation-Centric Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03659v1)
- **Authors**: Yinuo Wang, Robert E. Mercer, Frank Rudzicz, Sudipta Singha Roy, Pengjie Ren, Zhumin Chen, Xindi Wang
- **Abstract**: Trustworthiness in healthcare question-answering (QA) systems is important for ensuring patient safety, clinical effectiveness, and user confidence. As large language models (LLMs) become increasingly integrated into medical settings, the reliability of their responses directly influences clinical decision-making and patient outcomes. However, achieving comprehensive trustworthiness in medical QA poses significant challenges due to the inherent complexity of healthcare data, the critical nature of clinical scenarios, and the multifaceted dimensions of trustworthy AI. In this survey, we systematically examine six key dimensions of trustworthiness in medical QA, i.e., Factuality, Robustness, Fairness, Safety, Explainability, and Calibration. We review how each dimension is evaluated in existing LLM-based medical QA systems. We compile and compare major benchmarks designed to assess these dimensions and analyze evaluation-guided techniques that drive model improvements, such as retrieval-augmented grounding, adversarial fine-tuning, and safety alignment. Finally, we identify open challenges-such as scalable expert evaluation, integrated multi-dimensional metrics, and real-world deployment studies-and propose future research directions to advance the safe, reliable, and transparent deployment of LLM-powered medical QA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an evaluation-centric survey of trustworthiness in medical question answering (QA) systems based on large language models (LLMs). It systematically examines six key dimensions of trustworthiness: Factuality, Robustness, Fairness, Safety, Explainability, and Calibration. For each dimension, the survey reviews evaluation methods, existing benchmarks, and optimization techniques.  The paper identifies open challenges such as scalable expert evaluation, integrated multi-dimensional metrics, and real-world deployment studies, proposing future research directions to improve the safe and reliable deployment of LLM-powered medical QA.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its comprehensive, evaluation-driven framework for analyzing trustworthiness in medical QA. While individual dimensions of trustworthiness have been studied separately, the paper attempts to provide a more holistic view, explicitly connecting evaluation findings to model improvements. This approach, though conceptually straightforward, offers a valuable organizing principle for the field.  The emphasis on inter-dimensional relationships and tradeoffs is also a positive point, though it requires more in-depth exploration.

* **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the critical need for trustworthy medical QA systems.  Given the increasing integration of LLMs in healthcare, ensuring their reliability is paramount.  By systematically examining evaluation methods and benchmarks, the survey provides a roadmap for researchers and practitioners to build safer and more reliable systems.  However, the survey leans heavily on summarizing existing work, and provides limited groundbreaking insights of its own. The discussion of open challenges and future directions is somewhat generic. A more in-depth analysis of the limitations of current approaches and more concrete proposals for addressing these limitations would have significantly increased the paper's impact.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Coverage:**  The survey covers a broad range of trustworthiness dimensions and related evaluation techniques.
    * **Evaluation-Driven Perspective:** The explicit focus on linking evaluation to model improvements is a valuable contribution.
    * **Clear Organization:** The paper is well-organized and easy to follow.
    * **Relevant to Current Trends:** Addresses a very important and rapidly evolving area of research.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Original Insights:** The paper primarily summarizes existing work, lacking deep analytical insights or novel contributions to the field.
    * **Superficial Analysis of Inter-Dimensionality:** While the paper mentions the interplay between trustworthiness dimensions, the analysis remains relatively superficial.
    * **Generic Future Directions:** The discussion of open challenges and future research directions is somewhat generic and lacks concrete proposals.
    * **Limited Critical Depth:** The survey could benefit from a more critical analysis of the limitations of current benchmarks and evaluation techniques.
    * **Lack of a Unified Framework:** The survey discusses various aspects of trustworthiness. There is no presentation of a specific comprehensive framework that integrates all these aspects for evaluation.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper could serve as a useful resource for researchers and practitioners entering the field of trustworthy medical QA. It provides a good overview of the key dimensions of trustworthiness and related evaluation methods. However, its limited novelty and lack of deep insights may limit its long-term impact on the field.

* **Rigorous Rationale:**
The paper offers a useful synthesis of the area but its impact is limited by its lack of deeper insight and novel frameworks. Given the importance of the topic, the potential impact could have been much higher. While comprehensively covering the aspects, the paper is a summary of what is already in literature.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reason from Future: Reverse Thought Chain Enhances LLM Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03673v1)
- **Authors**: Yinlong Xu, Yanzhao Zheng, Shuoshuo Sun, Shuaihan Huang, Baohua Dong, Hangcheng Zhu, Ruohui Huang, Gang Yu, Hongxia Xu, Jian Wu
- **Abstract**: It has been demonstrated that carefully designed reasoning paradigms, like Chain-of-Thought (CoT) and Tree-of-Thought (ToT), can enhance the reasoning capabilities of small language models by detailed thinking and extensive thought searching, unbounded branching factors in the searching space create prohibitive reasoning consumption. However these methods fall into the trap of local optimum reasoning, which means the model lacks a global perspective while solving problems. We propose a novel reasoning paradigm called Reason from Future (RFF), which generates reasoning paths by bidirectional reasoning that combines top-down planning with bottom-up reasoning accumulation. The essence of RFF lies in its reverse reasoning mechanism, which prioritizes core logical relationships and imposes goal-oriented constraints on intermediate steps, thereby reducing the searching space and mitigating error accumulation inherent in sequential forward reasoning. Empirical evaluations across diverse experiments demonstrate that RFF outperforms conventional paradigms with higher accuracy and less searching space to solve complex tasks.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel reasoning paradigm for large language models (LLMs) called "Reason from Future" (RFF).  RFF combines bidirectional reasoning, interleaving top-down planning (reasoning backward from the desired outcome) with bottom-up reasoning accumulation (reasoning forward from the input).  The goal is to provide global guidance and reduce the search space during reasoning, mitigating local optima and error accumulation common in chain-of-thought (CoT) and tree-of-thought (ToT) approaches.  The paper empirically evaluates RFF on several benchmarks, including Game of 24, GSM8K, ASDiv, SVAMP, and MATH-500, demonstrating improved accuracy and reduced computational cost (measured by "visited states") compared to baselines like CoT, ToT, CR, and other related methods.  The paper also explores the robustness of RFF to variants in problems as well as data leakage by performing experiments on the symbolic variant of the GSM8K dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of RFF – combining backward and forward reasoning in an iterative manner – is genuinely novel.  While backward chaining and goal-oriented planning are established AI concepts, the way they are integrated with LLMs to guide reasoning steps is a significant contribution.
*   **Empirical Results:**  The experimental results are compelling.  RFF consistently outperforms strong baselines across a diverse set of reasoning benchmarks. The focus on "visited states" as a metric of efficiency is valuable, providing insight into the computational savings of RFF. The additional tests regarding robustness and data leakage risks are particularly important.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the RFF paradigm, its components (last step generator, stepwise forward reasoner, state check), and its implementation details.  The algorithm descriptions are helpful. The inclusion of Figure 1 and Figure 3 are quite helpful in visualizing the paradigm.
*   **Addressing limitations of existing methods:** The paper effectively critiques the limitations of purely forward reasoning approaches like CoT and ToT, highlighting the risk of getting trapped in local optima and the lack of global perspective. RFF provides a potential solution.
*   **Insightful analysis:**  The paper explores the effectiveness of the proposed approach and presents some helpful insights, such as the reasons CR may lead to overthinking.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on Reverse Thinking Quality:** The effectiveness of RFF heavily relies on the model's ability to generate correct "last steps" or high quality backwards reasoning. If the model struggles with generating appropriate target states, the entire paradigm could be undermined.  The paper acknowledges this in the "Limitations" section but doesn't explore it extensively.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** While the empirical results are strong, the paper lacks a strong theoretical justification for why RFF works so well.  A more formal analysis of the search space reduction or the convergence properties of the iterative process would strengthen the contribution. The paper explains the intuition behind the method, but doesn't provide a rigorous understanding of why RFF consistently reduces the search space.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The paper mentions some details regarding the parameters. But the performance is still dependent on the parameters. The best performance in the different tasks is observed with different settings, and there's no guidance provided on how to tune these parameters based on the type of problem.
*   **Limited analysis on types of problems:** While the set of datasets covers a variety of tasks, the paper doesn't provide clear guidance on which types of problems are most suitable for RFF and when a simple forward reasoning technique would work better. A more careful analysis of the types of reasoning processes where global guidance is most crucial would be helpful.
*   **Scalability and Practicality:**  Although the paper shows that RFF reduces the searching space compared to conventional paradigms, the method still requires multiple calls to the LLM to decompose one target state. And there isn't a clear discussion about the real-world scalability and cost-effectiveness of RFF, especially for tasks that require very complex or long reasoning chains.

**Significance:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the growing field of LLM reasoning. RFF offers a promising alternative to existing methods by incorporating goal-oriented planning and bidirectional reasoning. If the robustness and scalability of RFF can be further demonstrated, it has the potential to significantly improve the ability of LLMs to solve complex tasks more efficiently.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

The paper demonstrates a novel and well-executed idea with compelling empirical results. RFF is a conceptually significant advancement in LLM reasoning, offering a way to integrate global information with detailed inference steps. While the paper acknowledges limitations concerning theoretical justification and the quality of reverse thinking, the potential for improved accuracy and efficiency warrants a relatively high score. There are several areas for future research that would solidify the contribution of the paper, but the foundational ideas merit recognition.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Robust Preference Optimization via Dynamic Target Margins
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.03690v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Sun, Junkang Wu, Jiancan Wu, Zhibo Zhu, Xingyu Lu, Jun Zhou, Lintao Ma, Xiang Wang
- **Abstract**: The alignment of Large Language Models (LLMs) is crucial for ensuring their safety and reliability in practical applications. Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) has emerged as an efficient method that directly optimizes models using preference pairs, significantly reducing resource demands. However, the effectiveness of DPO heavily depends on the data quality, which is frequently compromised by noise. In this work, we propose $\gamma$-PO, a dynamic target margin preference optimization algorithm that adjust reward margins at the pairwise level. By introducing instance-specific margin calibration, $\gamma$-PO strategically prioritizes high-confidence pairs (those demonstrating higher reward margins) while suppressing potential noise from ambiguous pairs. Moreover, $\gamma$-PO is a plug-and-play method, compatible with variants of DPO that rely on reward margin between preference pairs. Across benchmarks such as AlpacaEval2 and Arena-Hard, $\gamma$-PO achieves an average 4.4\% improvement over other baselines, setting new benchmarks for state-of-the-art performance. Additionally, $\gamma$-PO requires minimal code changes and has a negligible impact on training efficiency, making it a robust solution for enhancing LLMs alignment. Our codes are available at \href{https://github.com/sunjie279/gammaPO}{https://github.com/sunjie279/gammaPO}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces γ-PO, a novel preference optimization algorithm designed to improve the robustness of Large Language Models (LLMs) alignment, particularly in the presence of noisy or ambiguous preference data. γ-PO is a "plug-and-play" method that dynamically adjusts target margins in Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) based on the confidence level of preference pairs. It increases target margins for high-confidence pairs (those with large reward differences) and decreases them for ambiguous pairs (those with small reward differences). This adaptive margin scaling mitigates the impact of noisy labels and strengthens learning from reliable preferences. The method can be applied to existing DPO variants, as demonstrated with γ-DPO and γ-SimPO. The paper presents experimental results across multiple benchmarks and base models, showing that γ-PO consistently outperforms other baseline methods, including DPO, SimPO, and rDPO. The authors also provide a theoretical connection between γ-PO and dynamic label smoothing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamically adjusting target margins based on confidence is a solid and practical contribution. While the connection to dynamic label smoothing exists, the explicit implementation and application within the DPO framework appear novel. Existing methods either apply uniform loss functions (ignoring confidence) or use rule-based filtering which has strong theoretical assumption. This work fills the gap by designing instance-aware algorithm which strategically prioritizes high-confidence pairs (demonstrating higher reward margins) while suppressing potential noise from ambiguous pairs.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of DPO: its sensitivity to noisy preference data, which is common in real-world applications. The proposed γ-PO method provides a robust and practical solution to mitigate this issue, leading to improved alignment performance.  The "plug-and-play" nature of γ-PO makes it easily adaptable to existing DPO-based alignment pipelines, increasing its potential for impact. The consistent performance gains across various models and benchmarks further highlight its significance. The paper is well-written and clearly presents the method, its theoretical justification, and experimental evaluation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is simple to implement and has low computational overhead.
    *   The "plug-and-play" nature of the algorithm allows it to be easily integrated with existing DPO pipelines.
    *   The experimental results consistently demonstrate improved performance across multiple benchmarks and base models.
    *   The paper provides a theoretical connection between γ-PO and dynamic label smoothing, providing a good theoretical foundation for the method.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method introduces an additional hyperparameter (τ), which requires tuning. Although the authors provide a search strategy, reducing or eliminating this hyperparameter would improve the method's usability. Future efforts will focus on eliminating this parameter, aiming to compute the adaptive margin automatically using existing parameters.
    *   The evaluation benchmarks, while standard, have known limitations. Incorporating more diverse and robust benchmarks and develop benchmarks that are more robust to parameter variations could provide a more comprehensive assessment of model performance.
