- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Generative Adaptive Replay Continual Learning Model for Temporal Knowledge Graph Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04083v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiyu Zhang, Wei Chen, Youfang Lin, Huaiyu Wan
- **Abstract**: Recent Continual Learning (CL)-based Temporal Knowledge Graph Reasoning (TKGR) methods focus on significantly reducing computational cost and mitigating catastrophic forgetting caused by fine-tuning models with new data. However, existing CL-based TKGR methods still face two key limitations: (1) They usually one-sidedly reorganize individual historical facts, while overlooking the historical context essential for accurately understanding the historical semantics of these facts; (2) They preserve historical knowledge by simply replaying historical facts, while ignoring the potential conflicts between historical and emerging facts. In this paper, we propose a Deep Generative Adaptive Replay (DGAR) method, which can generate and adaptively replay historical entity distribution representations from the whole historical context. To address the first challenge, historical context prompts as sampling units are built to preserve the whole historical context information. To overcome the second challenge, a pre-trained diffusion model is adopted to generate the historical distribution. During the generation process, the common features between the historical and current distributions are enhanced under the guidance of the TKGR model. In addition, a layer-by-layer adaptive replay mechanism is designed to effectively integrate historical and current distributions. Experimental results demonstrate that DGAR significantly outperforms baselines in reasoning and mitigating forgetting.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Deep Generative Adaptive Replay (DGAR) method for continual learning (CL) in Temporal Knowledge Graph Reasoning (TKGR). DGAR addresses the problem of catastrophic forgetting when TKGR models are updated with new data over time.  The method focuses on generating and adaptively replaying historical entity distributions from the whole historical context, rather than replaying individual facts. This is achieved through: 1) Historical Context Prompts (HCPs) which capture the historical context information; 2) Diffusion-Enhanced Historical Distribution Generation (Diff-HDG), which uses a diffusion model to generate historical distributions, enhancing common features with the current data distribution; and 3) a layer-by-layer Deep Adaptive Replay (DAR) mechanism that integrates the historical and current distributions. The paper presents experimental results on standard TKGR datasets, demonstrating that DGAR outperforms existing CL-based TKGR methods in both reasoning accuracy and mitigation of catastrophic forgetting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its specific approach to applying generative replay within the TKGR continual learning context. The idea of using diffusion models for generative replay isn't entirely new, but applying it to generate *entity distributions* conditioned on historical context within TKGR is a contribution. Constructing Historical Context Prompts and the layer-by-layer DAR mechanism are further innovative elements.  The approach combines techniques in a novel way within this domain.

* **Significance:**  TKGR is an important area with many real-world applications. Continual learning is crucial in scenarios where KGs are dynamic and constantly evolving. Catastrophic forgetting is a major bottleneck in continual learning, and DGAR provides a promising solution. The performance gains demonstrated in the experiments suggest the method has real potential. The ablation studies offer valuable insights into the contributions of the individual components of DGAR.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Results:**  The experiments provide compelling evidence that DGAR significantly outperforms existing methods across multiple datasets and metrics.
    * **Well-Designed Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the importance of each component of DGAR, providing justification for the design choices.
    * **Clear Problem Definition and Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of catastrophic forgetting in TKGR and motivates the need for a generative replay approach.
    * **Comprehensive Description of the Method:** The DGAR method is described in detail, making it relatively easy to understand and potentially reproduce.
    * **The code availability enhances reproducibility and adoption of the work.**

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty of Diffusion Model Application:** While the application is novel within TKGR, the core idea of using diffusion models for generative replay draws heavily from existing literature in other domains. The guiding mechanism by TKGR model parameters also feels like a heuristic rather than a theoretically well-motivated approach.
    * **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges that DGAR is more computationally expensive than simpler methods due to the diffusion model and DAR mechanism. While the performance gains may justify the added cost, this is a practical limitation. Though a parallel generation method is described, more comprehensive analysis of scalability would be beneficial.
    * **Limited Handling of New Entities and Relations:** The paper admits that the treatment of new entities and relations is simplistic. A more sophisticated approach to handling these is needed for broader applicability.
    * **The justification for certain design choices (e.g., the specific Transformer architecture) could be strengthened with more in-depth discussion and potentially alternative comparisons.** The choice of pre-trained model isn't rigorously justified.

* **Potential Influence:** If the code is released, the influence could be significant. Other researchers could adapt and extend DGAR to different TKGR tasks and datasets. The approach of generating entity distributions based on historical context could inspire new directions in CL for KGs.  The work is clearly presented, well-executed, and addresses an important problem, suggesting a positive influence on the field.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of continual learning for temporal knowledge graphs. The method is innovative, the experiments are thorough, and the results are promising. However, the relatively incremental nature of the novelty, the computational cost, and the limitations in handling new entities prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking paper.  The reliance on established techniques (diffusion models, Transformers) while effective, lowers the score. Therefore, a score of 7 is assigned to reflect the balance of these factors. The work improves the state of the art in a well-defined problem area, but doesn't represent a paradigm shift.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multimodal Tabular Reasoning with Privileged Structured Information
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04088v1)
- **Authors**: Jun-Peng Jiang, Yu Xia, Hai-Long Sun, Shiyin Lu, Qing-Guo Chen, Weihua Luo, Kaifu Zhang, De-Chuan Zhan, Han-Jia Ye
- **Abstract**: Tabular reasoning involves multi-step information extraction and logical inference over tabular data. While recent advances have leveraged large language models (LLMs) for reasoning over structured tables, such high-quality textual representations are often unavailable in real-world settings, where tables typically appear as images. In this paper, we tackle the task of tabular reasoning from table images, leveraging privileged structured information available during training to enhance multimodal large language models (MLLMs). The key challenges lie in the complexity of accurately aligning structured information with visual representations, and in effectively transferring structured reasoning skills to MLLMs despite the input modality gap. To address these, we introduce TabUlar Reasoning with Bridged infOrmation ({\sc Turbo}), a new framework for multimodal tabular reasoning with privileged structured tables. {\sc Turbo} benefits from a structure-aware reasoning trace generator based on DeepSeek-R1, contributing to high-quality modality-bridged data. On this basis, {\sc Turbo} repeatedly generates and selects the advantageous reasoning paths, further enhancing the model's tabular reasoning ability. Experimental results demonstrate that, with limited ($9$k) data, {\sc Turbo} achieves state-of-the-art performance ($+7.2\%$ vs. previous SOTA) across multiple datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the task of tabular reasoning, specifically when only table images are available at inference time, a more realistic scenario than having structured tables. It introduces TURBO (TabUlar Reasoning with Bridged information), a framework that leverages privileged structured tables during training to enhance the reasoning capabilities of multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  TURBO generates structure-aware reasoning traces using DeepSeek-R1, creating high-quality data to bridge the modality gap between structured tables and images. It then uses supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to iteratively refine the model's reasoning skills, achieving state-of-the-art performance with limited training data. The key idea is to transfer the rich semantics and precise structure of structured tables (available only during training) to the MLLM, enabling it to reason effectively over table images.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its approach to bridging the modality gap in tabular reasoning by using privileged information (structured tables only available during training). While the individual components (SFT, reinforcement learning with relative policy optimization) are not entirely novel, their specific combination and application to this problem, particularly with the structure-aware reasoning trace generation, constitutes a significant contribution. The idea of generating high-quality reasoning traces using a powerful LLM (DeepSeek-R1) and then using these traces to train an MLLM for table reasoning is also novel. The overall pipeline to improve the multimodal reasoning with limited data using structure tables in training is novel.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a practical problem: reasoning over table images, which are more common than structured tables in real-world scenarios. The performance improvements demonstrated by TURBO are substantial, showcasing the effectiveness of the proposed framework. The experimental results across multiple datasets strengthen the significance of the work. The paper also highlights the importance of interpretability by focusing on generating clear reasoning traces. This is highly valuable for debugging and understanding the model's decision-making process. The code and training pipeline may allow for more interpretable frameworks for real-world tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Novel framework combining reasoning trace generation, SFT, and RL.
    *   Strong experimental results, outperforming existing baselines by a significant margin, especially with limited data.
    *   Emphasis on interpretability and explainability.
    *   Comprehensive ablation studies validating the contributions of individual components.
    *   Careful consideration of experimental setup, including data sampling and hyperparameter tuning.
    *   Ablation studies confirm the important roles of each stage (SFT and RL).

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on DeepSeek-R1 for reasoning trace generation. While this leverages a powerful LLM, it also introduces a dependency and potential bias. The experiments depend a lot on the reasoning from DeepSeek and whether it can generate reliable responses.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the types of errors made by TURBO and the remaining challenges in tabular reasoning.
    *   The evaluation does not consider other metrics that are commonly used in structured data reasoning.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of tabular reasoning. The idea of using privileged structured information for training MLLMs could be applied to other tasks where there's a modality gap. The framework provides a solid foundation for future research on improving the reasoning capabilities of MLLMs for real-world applications.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign this paper a score of **8**. The paper presents a well-designed and effective framework for a practical problem, demonstrating significant performance improvements with limited data. The idea of bridging modalities with privileged information and the emphasis on interpretability are valuable contributions. While there are limitations, such as the dependency on DeepSeek-R1, they do not diminish the overall impact of the work. The code will contribute to further research.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### AmbiK: Dataset of Ambiguous Tasks in Kitchen Environment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04089v1)
- **Authors**: Anastasiia Ivanova, Eva Bakaeva, Zoya Volovikova, Alexey K. Kovalev, Aleksandr I. Panov
- **Abstract**: As a part of an embodied agent, Large Language Models (LLMs) are typically used for behavior planning given natural language instructions from the user. However, dealing with ambiguous instructions in real-world environments remains a challenge for LLMs. Various methods for task ambiguity detection have been proposed. However, it is difficult to compare them because they are tested on different datasets and there is no universal benchmark. For this reason, we propose AmbiK (Ambiguous Tasks in Kitchen Environment), the fully textual dataset of ambiguous instructions addressed to a robot in a kitchen environment. AmbiK was collected with the assistance of LLMs and is human-validated. It comprises 1000 pairs of ambiguous tasks and their unambiguous counterparts, categorized by ambiguity type (Human Preferences, Common Sense Knowledge, Safety), with environment descriptions, clarifying questions and answers, user intents, and task plans, for a total of 2000 tasks. We hope that AmbiK will enable researchers to perform a unified comparison of ambiguity detection methods. AmbiK is available at https://github.com/cog-model/AmbiK-dataset.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "AmbiK: Dataset of Ambiguous Tasks in Kitchen Environment" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AmbiK, a new fully textual dataset designed to benchmark ambiguity detection methods for embodied AI agents operating in kitchen environments. The dataset consists of 2000 paired ambiguous and unambiguous task instructions, along with environment descriptions, clarifying questions and answers, user intents, and task plans. Ambiguous tasks are categorized into three types: Human Preferences, Common Sense Knowledge, and Safety. The authors evaluate several state-of-the-art ambiguity detection methods, including CP-based approaches and simpler baselines, using various Large Language Models (LLMs). They demonstrate that AmbiK presents a significant challenge for existing methods and that LLM logits are likely inadequate approximations of uncertainty. The dataset and associated resources are publicly available to facilitate further research in this area.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper makes a contribution by creating a specific dataset focusing on ambiguous natural language instructions tailored for embodied AI. While datasets with some ambiguity exist, AmbiK distinguishes itself by its dedicated focus on this problem, the categorization of ambiguity types based on knowledge requirements, and the inclusion of clarifying information. This is more structured than prior work and better suited to drive specific algorithmic development. The application of LoFreeCP to embodied agent tasks also represents a minor novel element.

*   **Significance:** Addressing ambiguity is a crucial aspect of developing robust and reliable embodied AI agents. These agents need to handle vague instructions and request clarifications in order to perform tasks effectively and safely. AmbiK provides a standardized benchmark for comparing and evaluating ambiguity detection methods, potentially accelerating progress in this area. The work also suggests that directly utilizing logits from LLMs is insufficient for robust ambiguity detection, which is an important finding with implications for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Focused Dataset:** AmbiK is specifically designed for ambiguity detection, unlike general-purpose datasets with incidental ambiguous examples.
    *   **Comprehensive Annotations:** Includes environment descriptions, clarifying questions/answers, user intents, and task plans.
    *   **Clear Categorization:**  Divides ambiguity into meaningful types (Preferences, Common Sense, Safety) that relate to expected robot behavior.
    *   **Publicly Available:** The dataset is openly accessible, enabling widespread adoption and community-driven development.
    *   **Strong Baselines:** Evaluates several strong LLMs and fewshot detection methods including novel use of LoFreeCP.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Text-Only Representation:** Relies solely on textual descriptions of the environment, which can be less informative than visual or other sensory inputs. While the focus is on LLMs, this limits the transferability of the dataset to systems that integrate visual or more complex environment knowledge.
    *   **Limited Domain:** Restricted to kitchen environments, which may not generalize to other embodied AI applications.
    *   **Single Intent per Ambiguous Task:** Designed for tasks with a single "correct" interpretation, whereas real-world ambiguity can involve multiple valid possibilities.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The evaluation metrics, while well-defined, are more concerned with *whether* to ask for help than the *quality* of the question asked, representing a narrow view of what makes ambiguity useful for an interactive AI.
    *   **LLM limitations:** The lack of success across all the models tested shows a critical weakness of applying LLMs to robotic agent tasks, while simultaneously emphasizing the challenge AmbiK presents.

*   **Potential Influence:** AmbiK has the potential to become a widely used benchmark in the field of embodied AI. It can facilitate the development of more robust and reliable ambiguity detection methods, ultimately leading to more capable and user-friendly robots.  The categorization of ambiguity types could also inform the design of more effective human-robot interaction strategies. Further, by emphasizing how much work still needs to be done in LLM uncertainty estimation, it provides a clear roadmap for progress.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

While the paper introduces a valuable resource in AmbiK, its limitations, especially those related to the text-only nature, limited domain, and simplified notion of ambiguity, need to be considered. The novelty lies in its targeted design for ambiguity detection, but the impact is somewhat constrained by these limitations. Existing state-of-the-art large language models performed poorly, highlighting a significant challenge but also indicating that the benchmark does reveal weaknesses of current approaches and has value for guiding the direction of future research.

- **Score**: 7/10

### TextAtari: 100K Frames Game Playing with Language Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04098v1)
- **Authors**: Wenhao Li, Wenwu Li, Chuyun Shen, Junjie Sheng, Zixiao Huang, Di Wu, Yun Hua, Wei Yin, Xiangfeng Wang, Hongyuan Zha, Bo Jin
- **Abstract**: We present TextAtari, a benchmark for evaluating language agents on very long-horizon decision-making tasks spanning up to 100,000 steps. By translating the visual state representations of classic Atari games into rich textual descriptions, TextAtari creates a challenging test bed that bridges sequential decision-making with natural language processing. The benchmark includes nearly 100 distinct tasks with varying complexity, action spaces, and planning horizons, all rendered as text through an unsupervised representation learning framework (AtariARI). We evaluate three open-source large language models (Qwen2.5-7B, Gemma-7B, and Llama3.1-8B) across three agent frameworks (zero-shot, few-shot chain-of-thought, and reflection reasoning) to assess how different forms of prior knowledge affect performance on these long-horizon challenges. Four scenarios-Basic, Obscured, Manual Augmentation, and Reference-based-investigate the impact of semantic understanding, instruction comprehension, and expert demonstrations on agent decision-making. Our results reveal significant performance gaps between language agents and human players in extensive planning tasks, highlighting challenges in sequential reasoning, state tracking, and strategic planning across tens of thousands of steps. TextAtari provides standardized evaluation protocols, baseline implementations, and a framework for advancing research at the intersection of language models and planning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "TextAtari: 100K Frames Game Playing with Language Agents" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TextAtari, a new benchmark for evaluating language agents in long-horizon sequential decision-making. TextAtari translates the visual state representations of classic Atari games into rich textual descriptions, creating a challenging environment that bridges sequential decision-making and natural language processing. The benchmark consists of approximately 100 tasks with varying complexity, action spaces, and planning horizons, reaching up to 100,000 steps. The authors evaluate open-source large language models (LLMs) and agent frameworks under different scenarios to assess how prior knowledge affects performance. They highlight a performance gap between language agents and humans and identify challenges in long-horizon tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Strength:** The primary novelty lies in the benchmark itself.  Existing benchmarks for language agents in sequential decision-making are often limited to relatively short horizons. TextAtari directly addresses this limitation by offering tasks that require reasoning over tens of thousands of steps.
    *   **Weakness:** The idea of converting visual states into text isn't entirely novel. Previous research has explored similar approaches in other domains. However, TextAtari's scale and focus on very long-horizon Atari games differentiates it. The reliance on AtariARI, an existing tool for converting game states, reduces the novelty of the overall approach.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Strength:**  Long-horizon reasoning is a critical bottleneck for developing truly autonomous agents. By providing a standardized benchmark, TextAtari facilitates research in this important area. The paper's analysis of LLM performance on TextAtari sheds light on the current limitations of these models and points to directions for future research. The use of distinct scenarios (Basic, Obscured, Manual Augmentation, Reference-based) provides a structured way to explore how different knowledge sources can be incorporated into agent decision-making.
    *   **Weakness:** The evaluation focuses on relatively small, open-source LLMs. While understandable given computational constraints, it would be more impactful to see how larger, more powerful models perform on the benchmark. The gains from complex reasoning frameworks are relatively weak compared to the performance gains from strong priors (e.g., manual/reference guides), suggesting the benchmark may not be sufficiently challenging or nuanced for current cutting-edge LLMs.  The authors admit computational cost is a limiting factor and reduce the horizon to 1000 steps for cost saving. The TextAtari task environment may prove to simply have too much information per step with diminishing returns when it comes to very large horizons. The choice of Atari games, while classic, could be seen as less relevant to real-world challenges compared to more complex simulated or real-world environments.

*   **Impact:**

    *   **Potential:** TextAtari has the potential to become a widely used benchmark for evaluating language agents in long-horizon decision-making.  It could drive research into more effective reasoning and planning algorithms, memory management, and the incorporation of prior knowledge.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper makes a solid contribution by providing a new benchmark tailored to a critical challenge. The benchmark is well-defined, with clear evaluation protocols and baseline implementations. The systematic evaluation of LLMs and agent frameworks is valuable. However, the limitations in model size, reliance on an existing tool for state representation, and the potential limitations of the Atari game environment itself prevent the paper from achieving a higher score.

### Rectified Sparse Attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04108v1)
- **Authors**: Yutao Sun, Tianzhu Ye, Li Dong, Yuqing Xia, Jian Chen, Yizhao Gao, Shijie Cao, Jianyong Wang, Furu Wei
- **Abstract**: Efficient long-sequence generation is a critical challenge for Large Language Models. While recent sparse decoding methods improve efficiency, they suffer from KV cache misalignment, where approximation errors accumulate and degrade generation quality. In this work, we propose Rectified Sparse Attention (ReSA), a simple yet effective method that combines block-sparse attention with periodic dense rectification. By refreshing the KV cache at fixed intervals using a dense forward pass, ReSA bounds error accumulation and preserves alignment with the pretraining distribution. Experiments across math reasoning, language modeling, and retrieval tasks demonstrate that ReSA achieves near-lossless generation quality with significantly improved efficiency. Notably, ReSA delivers up to 2.42$\times$ end-to-end speedup under decoding at 256K sequence length, making it a practical solution for scalable long-context inference. Code is available at https://aka.ms/ReSA-LM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Rectified Sparse Attention":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Rectified Sparse Attention (ReSA), a novel approach for efficient long-sequence generation in large language models (LLMs). ReSA combines block-sparse attention (for efficiency) with periodic dense rectification (to mitigate error accumulation in the KV cache, which degrades generation quality).  The method involves alternating between sparse decoding and occasional dense forward passes to refresh the KV cache.  Experiments across various tasks like math reasoning, language modeling, and retrieval demonstrate that ReSA achieves near-lossless generation quality with significantly improved efficiency, exhibiting speedups in end-to-end decoding, particularly for long sequences. The authors provide code for their method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of ReSA – combining sparse attention with periodic dense rectification – has some degree of novelty. Sparse attention methods are already established, but the idea of *periodically* correcting the accumulated errors in the KV cache through dense attention is a reasonable and potentially practical contribution. The specific implementation details, like the dynamic top-n block selection strategy, also contribute to the novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in its potential for practical application in real-world LLM deployments. The problem of efficient long-sequence generation is critical for LLMs, and any method that can improve efficiency *without* significantly sacrificing quality is valuable. The experiments demonstrating speedups (up to 2.42x) while maintaining near-lossless performance across multiple benchmarks are strong evidence of its potential impact. The use of a widely used architecture for evalutation increases the applicability of ReSA. The availability of code further facilitates adoption by the community.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the ReSA approach.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, covering a range of tasks and sequence lengths.
    *   The results are convincing, demonstrating that ReSA achieves a good balance between efficiency and quality.
    *   The kernel-level analysis provides insights into the sources of performance gains.
    *   The method is compatible with LLM serving systems and optimizations, such as continuous batching and chunked prefill. This emphasizes the practicality.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the idea of rectification is novel, the components it builds on (sparse attention, dense attention) are established techniques. The innovation stems from the *combination* and tuning rather than any groundbreaking individual advance.
    *   The choice of specific parameters (rectification frequency, sparsity ratio, block size) involves some degree of hyperparameter tuning, which might require adjustments for different models and datasets. The discussion around hyperparameter selection is somewhat limited and could benefit from more detailed guidance.
    *   The performance benefits might be less pronounced on shorter sequences.
    *   The impact on training costs (if ReSA were incorporated during training, rather than just inference) is not discussed. It's primarily an inference-focused technique.

*   **Potential Influence:** The simplicity and practical benefits of ReSA could lead to its widespread adoption in LLM inference pipelines. It offers a relatively straightforward way to improve efficiency without requiring extensive model retraining or architectural changes.

**Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a practical and well-evaluated approach to efficient long-sequence generation. While the core components are not revolutionary, the combination of sparse attention and dense rectification, along with the associated implementation details, provides a valuable contribution. The demonstrated speedups and near-lossless performance improvements make it a potentially impactful technique for real-world LLM deployments. It addresses a critical problem in the field and offers a relatively simple and readily implementable solution. However, there remains the question of hyperparameter tuning and the sensitivity of performance to different parameters. Further exploration with different sparse attention techniques in place of ReSA may bring even more novel and impactful results.

### Guided Speculative Inference for Efficient Test-Time Alignment of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04118v1)
- **Authors**: Jonathan Geuter, Youssef Mroueh, David Alvarez-Melis
- **Abstract**: We propose Guided Speculative Inference (GSI), a novel algorithm for efficient reward-guided decoding in large language models. GSI combines soft best-of-$n$ test-time scaling with a reward model $r(x,y)$ and speculative samples from a small auxiliary model $\pi_S(y\mid x)$. We provably approximate the optimal tilted policy $\pi_{\beta,B}(y\mid x) \propto \pi_B(y\mid x)\exp(\beta\,r(x,y))$ of soft best-of-$n$ under the primary model $\pi_B$. We derive a theoretical bound on the KL divergence between our induced distribution and the optimal policy. In experiments on reasoning benchmarks (MATH500, OlympiadBench, Minerva Math), our method achieves higher accuracy than standard soft best-of-$n$ with $\pi_S$ and reward-guided speculative decoding (Liao et al., 2025), and in certain settings even outperforms soft best-of-$n$ with $\pi_B$. The code is available at https://github.com/j-geuter/GSI .
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Guided Speculative Inference (GSI), a new algorithm designed to enhance the efficiency of reward-guided decoding in large language models (LLMs). GSI strategically integrates speculative decoding (using a smaller auxiliary model), soft best-of-n sampling, and a rejection sampling mechanism. It aims to approximate the optimal reward-regularized policy under the primary (larger) model. The authors provide theoretical guarantees, demonstrating that GSI provably approaches the optimal policy as the number of speculative samples increases.  The method is evaluated on mathematical reasoning benchmarks, showing improvements over standard soft best-of-n sampling and reward-guided speculative decoding, even surpassing the base model's soft best-of-n performance in certain cases.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates a clever combination of existing techniques (speculative decoding, soft best-of-n, rejection sampling) within a reward-guided inference framework. The novelty lies in the specific way these components are integrated, particularly the reward-tilted re-weighting of candidate samples and the rejection mechanism to ensure fidelity. The theoretical justification and KL-divergence bound contributes to the novelty by providing formal guarantees that current methods lack.

*   **Significance:** Enhancing the efficiency of reward-guided decoding has significant implications for deploying aligned LLMs in practice. GSI's ability to leverage a smaller auxiliary model to accelerate inference while maintaining (and even improving) performance compared to the larger model is practically valuable, especially considering the high computational costs of running LLMs. The empirical results on challenging reasoning benchmarks, where LLMs struggle, highlight GSI's potential for real-world applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical grounding:** The KL divergence bound provides a theoretical guarantee for the induced policy. This is a strong point, as many existing heuristic approaches lack such justification.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are conducted on challenging mathematical reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating clear performance improvements over baselines.
    *   **Efficient implementation:** The use of vLLM for model implementation and the minimal computational overhead for likelihood calculations enhance the practicality of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on Auxiliary Model Quality:** GSI relies heavily on the quality of the auxiliary model to adequately cover the support of the base model. The effectiveness hinges on the validity of the coverage assumption (3), and the paper doesn't extensively explore scenarios where this assumption might be violated or what strategies can be employed to mitigate this.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance of GSI is likely sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters such as beta (reward scaling), u (rejection threshold), and n (number of samples). While the paper mentions selecting u empirically, a more systematic analysis of the trade-offs involved would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   **Limited Scope of Datasets:** The evaluation is limited to mathematical reasoning datasets.  While these are challenging, it would be beneficial to assess GSI's performance on other types of tasks (e.g., generation, summarization) and alignment rewards (e.g., safety, harmlessness) to better understand its general applicability. The inference time is similar to RSD but has a lower acceptance rate, therefore there are trade-offs in practice between acceptance rate and reward performance. The claim about how one trades-off againnst accuracy vs. the rate should be explored further.

*   **Potential Impact:** GSI's efficient integration of speculative decoding and reward models provides a promising avenue for future research on aligning and deploying LLMs. The theoretical guarantees and empirical results could encourage further investigation of similar hybrid approaches. Future research may focus on extending GSI to other tasks, exploring different auxiliary models, and improving the robustness to hyperparameter settings.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

GSI makes a valuable contribution by combining speculative decoding with reward modeling and providing theoretical guarantees.  The empirical results are convincing on the tested benchmarks. However, the reliance on the auxiliary model quality, hyperparameter sensitivity, and the limited scope of evaluation prevent a higher score. The improvements over RSD are not as significant given the trade-off with acceptance and inference time, which limits the overall significance of the work. While the presented method is novel and significant, the paper can be strengthened by further analysis.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Recent Advances in Medical Image Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04129v1)
- **Authors**: Loan Dao, Ngoc Quoc Ly
- **Abstract**: Medical image classification is crucial for diagnosis and treatment, benefiting significantly from advancements in artificial intelligence. The paper reviews recent progress in the field, focusing on three levels of solutions: basic, specific, and applied. It highlights advances in traditional methods using deep learning models like Convolutional Neural Networks and Vision Transformers, as well as state-of-the-art approaches with Vision Language Models. These models tackle the issue of limited labeled data, and enhance and explain predictive results through Explainable Artificial Intelligence.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation with a novelty and significance score for the paper "Recent Advances in Medical Image Classification."

**Summary:**

The paper provides a comprehensive review of recent progress in medical image classification (MIC), a field crucial for diagnosis and treatment. It categorizes the advancements across three levels: basic models, task-specific models, and applications. The review covers traditional methods enhanced by deep learning models like CNNs and Vision Transformers, as well as state-of-the-art approaches using Vision-Language Models (VLMs). The paper highlights how these models address the issue of limited labeled data through techniques like few-shot and zero-shot learning, and how Explainable AI (XAI) enhances and explains predictive results.  It also discusses public databases and challenges. The paper aims to contribute to the development of more effective and reliable classification systems to improve patient care and outcomes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Broad Coverage:** The paper offers a well-structured and fairly comprehensive overview of the MIC landscape. The organization into three levels (basic models, task-specific, and applications) is logical and facilitates understanding.
    *   **Timeliness:** The review focuses on recent advancements (mostly 2020-2024), making it a valuable resource for researchers looking to understand the current state of the art.
    *   **Discussion of Key Techniques:** The paper correctly highlights important techniques like VLMs, transformers, few-shot learning, zero-shot learning, and XAI, which are indeed driving significant progress in the field.
    *   **Emphasis on Practical Challenges:**  The paper acknowledges practical challenges, such as data scarcity, data privacy, and model interpretability, which are critical considerations for deploying MIC systems in real-world clinical settings.
    *   **Tables and Comparisons:**  The inclusion of tables summarizing models, architectures, and performance metrics is helpful for quick reference and comparison.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Depth of Analysis:** While broad, the review sometimes lacks a deep critical analysis of the individual methods. The descriptions of specific techniques can be somewhat superficial. The "why" behind the success or failure of certain approaches could be explored more thoroughly.
    *   **Uneven Coverage:** Some areas are covered more extensively than others. For example, the discussion of specific applications could be expanded. The connection between specific problems and the proposed model solving the problem is also not emphasized well.
    *   **Incremental Novelty in Review:** While the paper synthesizes existing work, it doesn't present any novel insights or frameworks for understanding the field. It is primarily a descriptive review rather than an analytical one.
    *   **Lack of Clinical Validation Focus:** More emphasis on clinical validation and impact metrics would strengthen the evaluation. Discussion of real-world clinical outcomes and the acceptance of these methods in clinical practice needs to be emphasized more.
    *   **Limited Critical Assessment of Datasets:**  A more critical assessment of the strengths and limitations of the various datasets used in MIC research would be valuable, especially regarding potential biases.
    *   **Missing Comparison on the Computational Cost:** The trade-off between model accuracy and computational cost in MIC should be addressed.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's primary contribution is as a survey and synthesis of existing research. It doesn't introduce new algorithms, architectures, or theoretical insights. Its value lies in providing a reasonably up-to-date overview of the field.  However, many other survey papers on MIC already exist; this paper lacks a clear, unique angle that significantly differentiates it from existing literature. The significance is that it helps junior researches navigate the field.

*   **Potential Influence:**

The paper can serve as a useful starting point for researchers entering the MIC field. It provides a good overview of the main trends and challenges. However, its limited depth of analysis and incremental novelty will likely limit its long-term impact on the field. The paper is descriptive in nature, rather than providing any new direction of where the next innovation needs to come from.

**Score: 5**

**Justification:**

The paper is a reasonably well-organized and timely survey of recent advances in MIC. However, its limited depth of analysis, uneven coverage, lack of novel insights, and the existence of other similar review papers prevent it from being considered a truly significant contribution. While it's a useful resource for newcomers, its influence on the field will likely be limited due to its incremental nature and relative lack of originality. The weaknesses overshadow the strengths in terms of originality and depth, hence the assignment of a more moderate score.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 5/10

### TRiSM for Agentic AI: A Review of Trust, Risk, and Security Management in LLM-based Agentic Multi-Agent Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04133v1)
- **Authors**: Shaina Raza, Ranjan Sapkota, Manoj Karkee, Christos Emmanouilidis
- **Abstract**: Agentic AI systems, built on large language models (LLMs) and deployed in multi-agent configurations, are redefining intelligent autonomy, collaboration and decision-making across enterprise and societal domains. This review presents a structured analysis of Trust, Risk, and Security Management (TRiSM) in the context of LLM-based agentic multi-agent systems (AMAS). We begin by examining the conceptual foundations of agentic AI, its architectural differences from traditional AI agents, and the emerging system designs that enable scalable, tool-using autonomy. The TRiSM in the agentic AI framework is then detailed through four pillars governance, explainability, ModelOps, and privacy/security each contextualized for agentic LLMs. We identify unique threat vectors and introduce a comprehensive risk taxonomy for the agentic AI applications, supported by case studies illustrating real-world vulnerabilities. Furthermore, the paper also surveys trust-building mechanisms, transparency and oversight techniques, and state-of-the-art explainability strategies in distributed LLM agent systems. Additionally, metrics for evaluating trust, interpretability, and human-centered performance are reviewed alongside open benchmarking challenges. Security and privacy are addressed through encryption, adversarial defense, and compliance with evolving AI regulations. The paper concludes with a roadmap for responsible agentic AI, proposing research directions to align emerging multi-agent systems with robust TRiSM principles for safe, accountable, and transparent deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive review of Trust, Risk, and Security Management (TRiSM) tailored for Large Language Model (LLM)-based Agentic Multi-Agent Systems (AMAS). It argues that the increasing use of agentic AI introduces unique risks that traditional AI security frameworks cannot adequately address. The paper introduces a structured TRiSM framework encompassing four pillars: Explainability, ModelOps, Application Security, and Model Privacy. It provides a threat taxonomy specific to agentic AI, evaluates existing methods for each TRiSM pillar, and outlines future research directions for building trustworthy, secure, and compliant AMAS. The work aims to provide researchers, engineers, and policymakers with a unified resource to design, evaluate, and govern agentic AI systems, especially in high-stakes domains.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its structured application of the TRiSM framework to the relatively new and rapidly evolving field of LLM-based agentic AI. While TRiSM itself isn't a new concept, its focused contextualization and adaptation for AMAS is a valuable contribution. The unique threat taxonomy and the detailed mapping of existing methods onto the TRiSM pillars demonstrate the paper's original analytical work. However, the work does not present new methodologies for mitigating risks or enhancing trust. Instead, it provides a survey and classification of risks and mitigations.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in the literature by explicitly connecting AI safety, security, and governance principles to the specific challenges posed by agentic AI. Given the projected growth and increasing autonomy of AI agents, the TRiSM framework offers a timely and relevant structure for managing potential risks. The identification of unique threat vectors such as prompt infection and collusion adds practical value. The roadmap for future research provides actionable directions for the community, and highlights key challenges and opportunities.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive and well-structured review.
    *   Clear and concise definitions of key concepts.
    *   Relevant and timely focus on agentic AI.
    *   Practical and actionable recommendations.
    *   Addresses a crucial need for robust governance.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Primarily a survey and categorization, without substantial technical innovations.
    *   The literature search and selection process could be more rigorously detailed.
    *   The future research directions, while reasonable, lack specific depth in any one area. It’s broad strokes across multiple areas.
    *   No novel benchmark to evaluate the performance for a proposed framework.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence research and development efforts in agentic AI by promoting a holistic and proactive approach to security and governance. It can serve as a valuable reference for practitioners implementing AMAS and for policymakers developing relevant regulations. It provides a common ground for interdisciplinary discussions on responsible AI development.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a comprehensive and timely overview of TRiSM principles in the context of LLM-based agentic AI, its novelty is primarily in the structuring and application of existing concepts to a new problem space, rather than introducing groundbreaking technical innovations. The depth of analysis could be increased by providing quantitative analysis on the framework evaluation. Therefore, a score of 7 is appropriate, which acknowledges the paper's significant contribution in structuring the field and providing practical guidance. The paper addresses a real and urgent need, and lays a foundation for future work that should improve or demonstrate the advantages of a novel approach for managing the discussed risks.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### MMR-V: What's Left Unsaid? A Benchmark for Multimodal Deep Reasoning in Videos
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04141v1)
- **Authors**: Kejian Zhu, Zhuoran Jin, Hongbang Yuan, Jiachun Li, Shangqing Tu, Pengfei Cao, Yubo Chen, Kang Liu, Jun Zhao
- **Abstract**: The sequential structure of videos poses a challenge to the ability of multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to locate multi-frame evidence and conduct multimodal reasoning. However, existing video benchmarks mainly focus on understanding tasks, which only require models to match frames mentioned in the question (hereafter referred to as "question frame") and perceive a few adjacent frames. To address this gap, we propose MMR-V: A Benchmark for Multimodal Deep Reasoning in Videos. The benchmark is characterized by the following features. (1) Long-range, multi-frame reasoning: Models are required to infer and analyze evidence frames that may be far from the question frame. (2) Beyond perception: Questions cannot be answered through direct perception alone but require reasoning over hidden information. (3) Reliability: All tasks are manually annotated, referencing extensive real-world user understanding to align with common perceptions. (4) Confusability: Carefully designed distractor annotation strategies to reduce model shortcuts. MMR-V consists of 317 videos and 1,257 tasks. Our experiments reveal that current models still struggle with multi-modal reasoning; even the best-performing model, o4-mini, achieves only 52.5% accuracy. Additionally, current reasoning enhancement strategies (Chain-of-Thought and scaling test-time compute) bring limited gains. Further analysis indicates that the CoT demanded for multi-modal reasoning differs from it in textual reasoning, which partly explains the limited performance gains. We hope that MMR-V can inspire further research into enhancing multi-modal reasoning capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MMR-V: What's Left Unsaid? A Benchmark for Multimodal Deep Reasoning in Videos":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MMR-V, a new benchmark designed to evaluate the multimodal deep reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) on videos.  Unlike existing video benchmarks that often focus on basic understanding and perception, MMR-V emphasizes: (1) Long-range, multi-frame reasoning, (2) Reasoning beyond direct perception, requiring inference and implicit understanding, (3) Reliability with manual annotation and cross-referencing against user comments, and (4) Confusability through carefully crafted distractors to avoid model shortcuts. The dataset comprises 317 diverse videos with 1257 multiple-choice tasks, spanning various categories. Experiments demonstrate that current LLMs struggle on MMR-V, with even the best model achieving only 52.5% accuracy. The paper further explores the limitations of current reasoning enhancement strategies and identifies a significant gap between model and human performance, particularly in video reasoning tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in the video understanding research field. Existing benchmarks often focus on tasks like action recognition or video captioning, failing to adequately test higher-order reasoning and inference capabilities within videos. The emphasis on long-range dependencies, implicit understanding, and confusability distinguishes MMR-V from existing benchmarks. The idea of incorporating implicit reasoning, drawing inspiration from cognitive theories like Kahneman's Dual Process Theory, is a fresh approach. However, relying on user comments to generate questions risks introducing biases or limitations.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it:

    *   **Highlights the limitations of current LLMs for video reasoning:** It clearly demonstrates that current state-of-the-art models struggle with tasks requiring deeper understanding and inference in video, even when compared to their performance on image or text-based tasks.

    *   **Provides a challenging benchmark for future research:** MMR-V sets a higher bar for evaluating progress in multimodal reasoning. It can serve as a valuable resource for developing and testing new models and algorithms designed to better understand videos.

    *   **Offers insights into the challenges of multimodal reasoning:** The paper's analysis of model failures, particularly the reliance on text-based reasoning and limited ability to leverage long-range visual cues, provides valuable directions for future research. The CoT analysis is especially insightful in showing how it is different from textual reasoning.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem and clear motivation:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing benchmarks and provides a strong rationale for the need for a more challenging dataset.

    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The manual annotation process, the careful design of distractors, and the analysis of model failures contribute to the paper's credibility.

    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper evaluates a wide range of models, including both proprietary and open-source options.

    *   **Detailed analysis:** The error analysis provides specific insights into the types of reasoning errors made by current models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Annotation Bias:** While the paper attempts to mitigate subjective bias using popular video comments, this introduces a potential dependence on the specific cultural contexts and perspectives represented within those comments. The reliance on a single human interpretation even after reference to user consensus could be a source of variability.
    *   **Limited Video Categories:** While the dataset covers various video types, the underrepresentation of certain genres (mystery, gaming, etc.) potentially limits the generalizability of findings to a broader array of real-world scenarios.
    *   **English-Centric:** The strong English emphasis reduces direct applicability to multilingual contexts. Multilingual support could lead to a more robust evaluation.
    *   **No direct comparison to baselines:** The paper does not describe the process of choosing existing benchmarks (like Video-MME or MVBench) for comparison. There seems to be no direct comparison of the current models with those benchmarks, making it difficult to assess the relative difficulty and utility of the new benchmark.
    *   **Lack of ablation studies.** The analysis on the quality of questions and options based on using GPT-4 vs human annotators lacks a proper ablation study that tests different types of models.
    *   **No explicit guidelines:** The paper claims it can differentiate human performance from model performance, but does not give explicit guidelines or evaluation metrics that might be useful for future evaluations.

*   **Potential Influence:** MMR-V has the potential to significantly influence the field of multimodal video understanding. It can encourage researchers to develop models that go beyond simple perception and focus on deeper reasoning and inference capabilities.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7**. The paper makes a significant contribution by identifying the need for a more challenging video reasoning benchmark and providing a solid foundation for future research.  The effort to create a large, diverse dataset and the comprehensive analysis of model failures are commendable.  However, limitations in the annotation process, the video selection process, and the lack of explicit baseline comparison slightly reduce the score. The limitations do not undermine the value of MMR-V as a benchmark, but they represent areas for improvement in future iterations.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Establishing Trustworthy LLM Evaluation via Shortcut Neuron Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04142v1)
- **Authors**: Kejian Zhu, Shangqing Tu, Zhuoran Jin, Lei Hou, Juanzi Li, Jun Zhao
- **Abstract**: The development of large language models (LLMs) depends on trustworthy evaluation. However, most current evaluations rely on public benchmarks, which are prone to data contamination issues that significantly compromise fairness. Previous researches have focused on constructing dynamic benchmarks to address contamination. However, continuously building new benchmarks is costly and cyclical. In this work, we aim to tackle contamination by analyzing the mechanisms of contaminated models themselves. Through our experiments, we discover that the overestimation of contaminated models is likely due to parameters acquiring shortcut solutions in training. We further propose a novel method for identifying shortcut neurons through comparative and causal analysis. Building on this, we introduce an evaluation method called shortcut neuron patching to suppress shortcut neurons. Experiments validate the effectiveness of our approach in mitigating contamination. Additionally, our evaluation results exhibit a strong linear correlation with MixEval, a recently released trustworthy benchmark, achieving a Spearman coefficient ($\rho$) exceeding 0.95. This high correlation indicates that our method closely reveals true capabilities of the models and is trustworthy. We conduct further experiments to demonstrate the generalizability of our method across various benchmarks and hyperparameter settings. Code: https://github.com/GaryStack/Trustworthy-Evaluation
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of data contamination in large language model (LLM) evaluation, arguing that current benchmarks are unreliable due to models potentially memorizing or "shortcutting" solutions.  The authors propose a novel evaluation method called "shortcut neuron patching."  It involves identifying specific neurons responsible for shortcut reasoning by analyzing activation differences between contaminated and uncontaminated models, coupled with causal analysis via neuron patching.  These "shortcut neurons" are then suppressed during evaluation to reveal the model's true capabilities. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of this approach on mathematical reasoning benchmarks, showing a significant reduction in the performance of contaminated models, while minimizing impact on uncontaminated ones. Furthermore, the evaluation results correlate strongly with other trustworthy benchmarks, demonstrating the validity of the method. The method appears generalizable across models, datasets, and hyperparameters.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of targeting "shortcut neurons" to address data contamination is a significant and novel contribution. While neuron analysis and patching are existing techniques, their specific application to identify and suppress shortcuts for trustworthy LLM evaluation is innovative. The comparative and causal analysis to locate these neurons is also a well-defined and novel method.

* **Significance:** Data contamination is a major issue affecting the trustworthiness of LLM evaluations.  If the method presented can reliably mitigate this issue, the potential impact on the field is high. It provides a way to more accurately assess the true capabilities of LLMs, guiding development and deployment decisions. Demonstrating strong correlation with independently constructed trustworthy benchmarks further bolsters the significance. The generalizability claims are also significant if further confirmed.

* **Strengths:**
    * The paper clearly articulates the problem of data contamination and its negative impacts on LLM evaluations.
    * The proposed method is well-defined, with clear steps for identifying and patching shortcut neurons.
    * The empirical results are convincing, demonstrating the effectiveness of the method in mitigating contamination and preserving the performance of uncontaminated models.
    * The correlation with other trustworthy benchmarks strengthens the validity of the proposed method.
    * The paper addresses generalizability across models, benchmarks, and hyperparameters.
    * Code is provided, enhancing reproducibility.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The experiments are primarily focused on mathematical reasoning benchmarks.  While this is a common area for contamination, it is important to extend this method to other types of tasks (e.g., common sense reasoning, factual knowledge) to verify broader applicability. Although they briefly examine other scenarios in the appendix, further exploration is warranted.
    * The method relies on fine-tuning both contaminated and uncontaminated models to compare neuron activation patterns. This can be computationally expensive. A more efficient method for identifying shortcut neurons would improve the practicality of the approach.
    * The shortcut neuron patching is performed using activations from a "base model". The performance may then depend on how representative that base model's activations are.
    * The notion of a "shortcut" is somewhat implicitly defined as learned correlations between a model's training dataset and the downstream task. It would be useful to better characterize the nature of these shortcut neurons.
    * The selection of the top 5000 neurons for patching seems somewhat arbitrary. A more principled approach for determining the optimal number of neurons to patch would be beneficial.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence how LLMs are evaluated, particularly in scenarios where data contamination is a concern.  If validated and adopted more widely, it could become a standard technique for ensuring the trustworthiness of LLM benchmarks. The code release will likely spur further research and development in this area.

* **Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a novel method to tackle an important problem, data contamination. The empirical results appear robust and the findings are well supported, and the code is shared. The weaknesses relate to the limited scope of tasks considered, high computational overhead to identify short-cut neurons, and certain method-specific limitations. Taking these factors into account, I would assign this paper a score reflecting a solid, valuable contribution to the field but with scope for refinement and expansion.

Score: 7

- **Score**: 7/10

### A Dataset for Addressing Patient's Information Needs related to Clinical Course of Hospitalization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04156v1)
- **Authors**: Sarvesh Soni, Dina Demner-Fushman
- **Abstract**: Patients have distinct information needs about their hospitalization that can be addressed using clinical evidence from electronic health records (EHRs). While artificial intelligence (AI) systems show promise in meeting these needs, robust datasets are needed to evaluate the factual accuracy and relevance of AI-generated responses. To our knowledge, no existing dataset captures patient information needs in the context of their EHRs. We introduce ArchEHR-QA, an expert-annotated dataset based on real-world patient cases from intensive care unit and emergency department settings. The cases comprise questions posed by patients to public health forums, clinician-interpreted counterparts, relevant clinical note excerpts with sentence-level relevance annotations, and clinician-authored answers. To establish benchmarks for grounded EHR question answering (QA), we evaluated three open-weight large language models (LLMs)--Llama 4, Llama 3, and Mixtral--across three prompting strategies: generating (1) answers with citations to clinical note sentences, (2) answers before citations, and (3) answers from filtered citations. We assessed performance on two dimensions: Factuality (overlap between cited note sentences and ground truth) and Relevance (textual and semantic similarity between system and reference answers). The final dataset contains 134 patient cases. The answer-first prompting approach consistently performed best, with Llama 4 achieving the highest scores. Manual error analysis supported these findings and revealed common issues such as omitted key clinical evidence and contradictory or hallucinated content. Overall, ArchEHR-QA provides a strong benchmark for developing and evaluating patient-centered EHR QA systems, underscoring the need for further progress toward generating factual and relevant responses in clinical contexts.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a rigorous evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ArchEHR-QA, a novel dataset designed to address patient information needs related to their clinical course of hospitalization. The dataset comprises real-world patient questions from public health forums, clinician-interpreted counterparts, relevant EHR excerpts with sentence-level relevance annotations, and clinician-authored answers. The authors benchmarked the dataset using three open-weight large language models (LLMs) with different prompting strategies to establish performance baselines for grounded EHR question answering (QA), evaluating factuality and relevance. The results highlight the need for advancements in AI to improve factual consistency and relevance in clinical contexts.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution, but its novelty is somewhat limited. Here's a detailed breakdown:

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dataset Uniqueness:**  The primary strength is the creation of a focused, expert-annotated dataset specifically tailored to *patient*-centered EHR QA.  This fills a significant gap, as existing datasets largely concentrate on clinician needs. The combination of real patient questions, clinician interpretations, and relevance-annotated EHR excerpts is valuable.

    *   **Benchmarking:** The benchmarking provides initial performance baselines for LLMs on this task. The exploration of different prompting strategies is useful for understanding model behavior.

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation Metrics:** The use of both factuality and relevance metrics provides a more complete evaluation compared to focusing solely on text similarity.

    *   **Error Analysis:** Identifying common error types (omission of evidence, hallucination) is critical for guiding future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Size:** The dataset (134 cases) is relatively small. While expert-annotated, a larger dataset would provide more robust benchmarks and training data. This limits its immediate practical applicability for training high-performing models.

    *   **Limited LLM Comparison:** The benchmarking is restricted to a few open-weight LLMs. A more extensive evaluation including closed-source models (e.g., GPT-4) would provide a more comprehensive picture of the state-of-the-art.

    *   **Generalizability:** The dataset is derived from a single medical institution, which might limit its generalizability to other healthcare settings and EHR systems.

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the dataset *is* novel, the methodology for creating QA datasets and evaluating LLMs is reasonably standard. The paper doesn't introduce fundamentally new methods in data creation or evaluation. The alignment process between patient questions and clinical notes seems to have included augmenting both patient and physician text via Large Language Models, which introduces a reliance and potential perpetuation of whatever biases are inherent in the LLM.
    *   **Lack of a complete manual error analysis:** A more complete picture of the errors made by the model could inform future models and development. The high-level overview provided in the summary isn't enough.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in:

    *   **Focus on Patient Needs:**  Shifting the focus of EHR QA research to address patient information needs is a crucial step towards patient-centered care.

    *   **Foundation for Future Research:** ArchEHR-QA provides a valuable starting point for developing and evaluating AI systems that can help patients understand their EHR data.

    *   **Community Engagement:** The shared task highlights the community's interest in this area and promotes further innovation.

**Justification:**

The paper is a solid contribution, but its impact is limited by the dataset size and the incremental nature of the methodology. The focus on patient-centered QA is commendable, and the dataset is a useful resource. However, the evaluation is somewhat narrow in scope, and the findings aren't groundbreaking. The limitations in the method of alignment between the patient questions and physician notes is concerning.

### Image Editing As Programs with Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04158v1)
- **Authors**: Yujia Hu, Songhua Liu, Zhenxiong Tan, Xingyi Yang, Xinchao Wang
- **Abstract**: While diffusion models have achieved remarkable success in text-to-image generation, they encounter significant challenges with instruction-driven image editing. Our research highlights a key challenge: these models particularly struggle with structurally inconsistent edits that involve substantial layout changes. To mitigate this gap, we introduce Image Editing As Programs (IEAP), a unified image editing framework built upon the Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architecture. At its core, IEAP approaches instructional editing through a reductionist lens, decomposing complex editing instructions into sequences of atomic operations. Each operation is implemented via a lightweight adapter sharing the same DiT backbone and is specialized for a specific type of edit. Programmed by a vision-language model (VLM)-based agent, these operations collaboratively support arbitrary and structurally inconsistent transformations. By modularizing and sequencing edits in this way, IEAP generalizes robustly across a wide range of editing tasks, from simple adjustments to substantial structural changes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that IEAP significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods on standard benchmarks across various editing scenarios. In these evaluations, our framework delivers superior accuracy and semantic fidelity, particularly for complex, multi-step instructions. Codes are available at https://github.com/YujiaHu1109/IEAP.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Image Editing As Programs with Diffusion Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces IEAP (Image Editing As Programs), a novel framework for instruction-driven image editing using diffusion models, specifically built upon the Diffusion Transformer (DiT) architecture. The core idea is to decompose complex editing instructions into sequences of simpler, "atomic" operations. These atomic operations include RoI localization, RoI inpainting, RoI editing, RoI compositing, and global transformations. A vision-language model (VLM)-based agent programs these operations in sequence, which are then executed by a lightweight adapter sharing the DiT backbone. This modular approach allows for handling structurally inconsistent edits, which are a known weakness of current diffusion models. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance across various benchmarks, particularly for complex, multi-step instructions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The central novelty lies in the "decomposition" approach to image editing using diffusion models. While the individual components (DiT, VLM agents, atomic operations) are not entirely new, the combination of these elements into a cohesive system capable of handling structural inconsistencies is a significant advance. The idea of framing image editing as a program execution problem is also refreshing and offers a structured way to approach the complex task of instruction following.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical limitation in instruction-driven image editing – the difficulty of handling substantial layout changes. By modularizing the editing process, IEAP demonstrates a significant improvement in accuracy and semantic fidelity, particularly for complex instructions. The results presented are compelling and show that the proposed framework outperforms existing methods on standard benchmarks. The potential for IEAP to improve diverse applications is considerable, for example, image story telling and scientific visualizations.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing diffusion models for instruction-driven editing.

    *   **Innovative Approach:** The decomposition into atomic operations is a clever way to tackle the structural inconsistency problem.

    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental validation, demonstrating the effectiveness of IEAP across various editing scenarios. The visual results are convincing.
    * **Open-source**: The release of code further enhance the impact of the study

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** The paper acknowledges the significant per-operation latency, precluding real-time interactivity. This is a practical limitation.
    *   **Complex Scene Handling:** While IEAP addresses structural inconsistencies, it still struggles with highly dynamic scenes (motion blur) and complex physical interactions (lighting inconsistencies).
    *   **Resource intensive**: running DiT requires expensive hardware.

*   **Potential Impact:** The IEAP framework has the potential to influence future research in instruction-driven image editing. The modular design offers a clear direction for improvement and extension. Other researchers may adopt the "programming" approach to tackle other challenges in image generation and manipulation.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:**

    *   The paper's innovation score of 8 is based on the following arguments: First, by decomposing the problem to a set of atomic operations the method presents significant improvement on structural alteration tasks, one of the current challenges in image editing. Second, the work contributes strong empirical results. Third, the design of IEAP offers a clear direction to improvements for the research in image editing. One should notice that IEAP still has room for improvement in the performance of handling the interactions of complex scenes and that the reliance on DiT architectures comes with the price of relatively high computational costs.

**Score: 8**
