### Does Prompt Design Impact Quality of Data Imputation by LLMs?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04172v1)
- **Authors**: Shreenidhi Srinivasan, Lydia Manikonda
- **Abstract**: Generating realistic synthetic tabular data presents a critical challenge in machine learning. It adds another layer of complexity when this data contain class imbalance problems. This paper presents a novel token-aware data imputation method that leverages the in-context learning capabilities of large language models. This is achieved through the combination of a structured group-wise CSV-style prompting technique and the elimination of irrelevant contextual information in the input prompt. We test this approach with two class-imbalanced binary classification datasets and evaluate the effectiveness of imputation using classification-based evaluation metrics. The experimental results demonstrate that our approach significantly reduces the input prompt size while maintaining or improving imputation quality compared to our baseline prompt, especially for datasets that are of relatively smaller in size. The contributions of this presented work is two-fold -- 1) it sheds light on the importance of prompt design when leveraging LLMs for synthetic data generation and 2) it addresses a critical gap in LLM-based data imputation for class-imbalanced datasets with missing data by providing a practical solution within computational constraints. We hope that our work will foster further research and discussions about leveraging the incredible potential of LLMs and prompt engineering techniques for synthetic data generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the impact of prompt design on the quality of data imputation using Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically for class-imbalanced tabular datasets. The authors propose a token-aware data imputation method that leverages the in-context learning abilities of LLMs. This is achieved through a combination of a structured group-wise CSV-style prompting technique, and by carefully selecting only the most relevant features for the imputation task (i.e., those strongly correlated with the feature containing missing values), with the aim of minimizing prompt length and token usage. They evaluate the method on two class-imbalanced binary classification datasets, assessing imputation quality using classification-based evaluation metrics. The results demonstrate that their approach can reduce input prompt size while maintaining or improving imputation quality, particularly for smaller datasets.  The authors highlight the importance of prompt engineering for synthetic data generation using LLMs and address the gap in LLM-based data imputation for class-imbalanced datasets under computational constraints.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper has moderate novelty. While using LLMs for tabular data imputation is not entirely new (e.g., Borisov et al. 2022, Kim et al. 2024), the specific focus on prompt design for *class-imbalanced* data and the explicit goal of *reducing input token size through feature selection* are valuable contributions. The structured CSV-style prompting technique and the careful elimination of irrelevant features are the key novelties. The approach of selecting correlated features is logical but may be considered a common-sense optimization in the context of prompt engineering.
*   **Significance:** The significance lies in the practical application and demonstration of LLMs for a common data challenge: handling missing data in class-imbalanced scenarios. The findings are important because they provide a practical, computationally efficient method for data imputation, particularly relevant when computational resources are limited, and especially to help improve results with small datasets. The focus on minimizing token usage is crucial given the cost and length limitations of many LLM APIs. The work sheds light on how prompt engineering can significantly impact performance, influencing future research in this area. By showing the positive results from prompting strategies, the work provides practical solutions for handling missingness in tabular datasets within a resource-constrained environment.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies a practical problem (data imputation in class-imbalanced data) and limitations of existing approaches (resource-intensive fine-tuning).
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** The proposed method is well-explained and reproducible.  The use of CSV-style prompting and feature selection based on correlation is logical and easy to implement.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The experiments are well-designed, using established datasets and evaluation metrics.  The comparison to a baseline (ungrouped examples) is useful.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The work offers a concrete solution for practitioners who want to use LLMs for data imputation but are constrained by computational resources.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to two datasets.  A more diverse set of datasets, including those with different types of missingness, would strengthen the findings. The datasets used were also relatively small. Demonstrating the advantage with much larger datasets would improve credibility.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The improvement achieved by feature selection, while present, is not overwhelmingly large in all experiments.  The magnitude of the impact might be questioned.
    *   **Correlation as Sole Feature Selection Criterion:** Relying solely on correlation might overlook complex non-linear relationships between features. The methodology used to determine the correlation values could be improved (i.e. more sophisticated metrics than Pearson or Cramer's V). There are no comparison against more sophisticated feature selection techniques.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies (e.g., testing different numbers of examples in the prompt) would provide further insights into the impact of each component of the proposed method.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** There is no theoretical analysis to explain why the proposed prompt engineering approach works. This might improve the generality of the findings.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper will likely influence future research in prompt engineering for tabular data and data imputation, particularly in scenarios with limited resources and class imbalance. The practical guidelines for prompt design will be useful to practitioners. The token awareness method can be used in other different fields that require the use of LLMs.
*   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, despite a few grammatical errors. The method is clearly described.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a worthwhile contribution with a clear, practical method for improving LLM-based data imputation in class-imbalanced datasets. The token-aware approach and the emphasis on prompt design are valuable. However, the incremental nature of the improvements, the limited scope of the evaluation, and the reliance on a relatively simple feature selection method prevent a higher score. The novelty isn't groundbreaking, but the practical focus and the clear demonstration of the benefits of prompt engineering justify a score of 6.5. While the results are encouraging, more thorough empirical testing and analysis are needed to establish the method's robustness and generalizability.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### SkipGPT: Dynamic Layer Pruning Reinvented with Token Awareness and Module Decoupling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04179v1)
- **Authors**: Anhao Zhao, Fanghua Ye, Yingqi Fan, Junlong Tong, Zhiwei Fei, Hui Su, Xiaoyu Shen
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) achieve remarkable performance across tasks but incur substantial computational costs due to their deep, multi-layered architectures. Layer pruning has emerged as a strategy to alleviate these inefficiencies, but conventional static pruning methods overlook two critical dynamics inherent to LLM inference: (1) horizontal dynamics, where token-level heterogeneity demands context-aware pruning decisions, and (2) vertical dynamics, where the distinct functional roles of MLP and self-attention layers necessitate component-specific pruning policies. We introduce SkipGPT, a dynamic layer pruning framework designed to optimize computational resource allocation through two core innovations: (1) global token-aware routing to prioritize critical tokens, and (2) decoupled pruning policies for MLP and self-attention components. To mitigate training instability, we propose a two-stage optimization paradigm: first, a disentangled training phase that learns routing strategies via soft parameterization to avoid premature pruning decisions, followed by parameter-efficient LoRA fine-tuning to restore performance impacted by layer removal. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SkipGPT reduces over 40% of model parameters while matching or exceeding the performance of the original dense model across benchmarks. By harmonizing dynamic efficiency with preserved expressivity, SkipGPT advances the practical deployment of scalable, resource-aware LLMs. Our code is publicly available at: https://github.com/EIT-NLP/SkipGPT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "SkipGPT: Dynamic Layer Pruning Reinvented with Token Awareness and Module Decoupling" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SkipGPT, a dynamic layer pruning framework for large language models (LLMs). SkipGPT addresses the limitations of static pruning by considering two key dynamics: (1) *horizontal dynamics*, where token-level heterogeneity necessitates context-aware pruning, and (2) *vertical dynamics*, where MLP and self-attention layers require component-specific pruning. SkipGPT achieves this through (1) global token-aware routing, prioritizing critical tokens, and (2) decoupled pruning policies for MLP and self-attention. To ensure stable training, the paper proposes a two-stage optimization paradigm: a disentangled training phase followed by LoRA fine-tuning. The experiments show that SkipGPT reduces model parameters by over 40% while maintaining or improving the performance of the original dense model. Furthermore, router behaviour analysis provides insights into the redundancy of Attention vs MLP modules and how context length affects their respective roles.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its holistic approach to dynamic layer pruning.  While dynamic pruning and token-aware routing exist, SkipGPT uniquely combines both horizontal and vertical dynamics.  The two-stage training is a good practical improvement and addresses a key limitation that would likely arise with the joint training approaches.  The insights on the redundancy of attention modules and the impact of context length are interesting and potentially lead to future optimization. There's a solid justification for why a two-stage approach is better than end-to-end.

*   **Significance:**  LLMs' computational cost is a significant barrier to wider adoption.  A method that demonstrably reduces parameter count while maintaining or improving performance is highly significant.  SkipGPT offers a promising approach toward creating more scalable and efficient LLMs.  The ablations provide sufficient support for the proposed components. The performance boost compared to static methods is a positive development. Analyzing router decisions helps to validate claims.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly articulated problem and motivation. The inefficiencies of existing LLM architectures are well-established.
    *   Well-defined framework with clear explanations of the techniques used.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple models and benchmarks.
    *   The two-stage training paradigm is a practical and effective solution for stable training.
    *   The analysis of router behavior provides insightful information about LLM architectures.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The complexity of dynamic pruning introduces additional overhead, which while likely amortized, is not directly compared. There are also additional hyperparameters that need to be tuned (sparsity target and its corresponding coefficient).
    *   Although the models match or exceed performance with fewer parameters there is no mention of inference speedup or associated cost. This is a notable omission given it is one of the key motivating factors. A more compelling story could be told if speedups and actual reduction in inference costs was reported.
    *   Limited discussion of limitations and potential negative societal impacts. While the framework improves efficiency, potential biases or misuse of the pruned models remain relevant.

*   **Potential Influence:**  SkipGPT can influence the field by:
    *   Providing a practical framework for dynamic layer pruning.
    *   Inspiring further research on token-aware and component-specific pruning strategies.
    *   Guiding the design of more efficient LLM architectures.
    *   Encouraging the development of dynamic methods for resource allocation during inference.

*   **Areas for future work:**
    *   Direct comparisons of inference speed and compute cost reduction with baseline.
    *   Further exploration of the insights gleaned from router behavior analysis.
    *   Investigating different routing mechanisms and loss functions.
    *   Apply on instruction tuning setups for practical improvements.

**Score: 7.5**

*Rationale:* The paper makes a valuable contribution by combining horizontal and vertical dynamic pruning, resulting in notable improvements in LLM efficiency. The two-stage training and analyses add practical value to the framework. However, the paper could be strengthened by reporting actual runtime speeds and costs and a fuller discussion of limitations. Still, the presented framework and insights position SkipGPT as a solid advancement in resource-aware LLM deployment, supporting a significant score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SuperWriter: Reflection-Driven Long-Form Generation with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04180v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhao Wu, Yushi Bai, Zhiqiang Hu, Juanzi Li, Roy Ka-Wei Lee
- **Abstract**: Long-form text generation remains a significant challenge for large language models (LLMs), particularly in maintaining coherence, ensuring logical consistency, and preserving text quality as sequence length increases. To address these limitations, we propose SuperWriter-Agent, an agent-based framework designed to enhance the quality and consistency of long-form text generation. SuperWriter-Agent introduces explicit structured thinking-through planning and refinement stages into the generation pipeline, guiding the model to follow a more deliberate and cognitively grounded process akin to that of a professional writer. Based on this framework, we construct a supervised fine-tuning dataset to train a 7B SuperWriter-LM. We further develop a hierarchical Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) procedure that uses Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to propagate final quality assessments and optimize each generation step accordingly. Empirical results across diverse benchmarks demonstrate that SuperWriter-LM achieves state-of-the-art performance, surpassing even larger-scale baseline models in both automatic evaluation and human evaluation. Furthermore, comprehensive ablation studies demonstrate the effectiveness of hierarchical DPO and underscore the value of incorporating structured thinking steps to improve the quality of long-form text generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, adhering to the specified criteria:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SuperWriter-Agent, a framework designed to enhance the quality and consistency of long-form text generation by large language models (LLMs).  It tackles the limitations of current LLMs, which often struggle with coherence, logical consistency, and maintaining text quality as the sequence length increases. SuperWriter-Agent addresses this by incorporating explicit structured thinking—through planning and refinement stages—into the generation pipeline.  It trains a 7B SuperWriter-LM on a supervised fine-tuning dataset and employs a hierarchical Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) procedure that uses Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to optimize each generation step. Empirical results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance compared to even larger-scale baseline models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The framework introduces a valuable, structured approach to long-form generation that mimics the human writing process more closely than many existing methods. Explicitly integrating planning, writing, and refining steps is a significant step towards better coherence and consistency.
    *   **Methodology:** The use of hierarchical DPO with MCTS for optimizing each generation step is a well-designed and potentially impactful technique.  The creation of a specialized, thinking-oriented dataset is also a strength, addressing a recognized gap in current training corpora.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper demonstrates impressive empirical results across diverse benchmarks.  Achieving state-of-the-art performance with a 7B model is particularly noteworthy, suggesting efficiency and potential scalability.  The ablation studies further strengthen the claim that the hierarchical DPO and structured thinking steps contribute significantly to the model's performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Scope of Tasks:** While WritingBench is a solid benchmark, the paper should discuss the types of long-form generation tasks for which SuperWriter-Agent is most and least effective. Does it perform equally well on creative writing, technical documentation, and argumentative essays?  A more nuanced analysis would improve the evaluation.
    *   **Computational Cost:**  The use of MCTS and hierarchical DPO inevitably increases computational cost. The paper needs to quantify this overhead and discuss its implications for practical deployment, especially compared to simpler, single-pass generation methods.
    *   **Model Size Limitation:**  While achieving strong results with a 7B model is commendable, the paper should acknowledge the potential limitations imposed by the model size.  How does the performance scale with larger models, and what kinds of knowledge-intensive tasks might still require larger models regardless of the framework?

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper provides a practical and effective approach to address a key challenge in LLM research—producing high-quality, coherent, and consistent long-form text. The SuperWriter-Agent framework and training methodologies could influence the development of future generation models and improve the utility of LLMs for various applications.

**Justification for the Score:**

I assign a score of **8** to this paper.  It presents a novel and well-executed framework with strong empirical support and tackles a significant problem in the field. The strengths clearly outweigh the weaknesses. The work is both technically sound and practically relevant. However, acknowledging limitations related to task scope, computational cost, and model size would further enhance the paper. The assigned score reflects the fact that, while the paper represents a substantial contribution, there is room for further investigation of its capabilities and scalability.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Long or short CoT? Investigating Instance-level Switch of Large Reasoning Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04182v1)
- **Authors**: Ruiqi Zhang, Changyi Xiao, Yixin Cao
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancement of large reasoning models, long Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting has demonstrated strong performance on complex tasks. However, this often comes with a significant increase in token usage. In this paper, we conduct a comprehensive empirical analysis comparing long and short CoT strategies. Our findings reveal that while long CoT can lead to performance improvements, its benefits are often marginal relative to its significantly higher token consumption. Specifically, long CoT tends to outperform when ample generation budgets are available, whereas short CoT is more effective under tighter budget constraints. These insights underscore the need for a dynamic approach that selects the proper CoT strategy based on task context and resource availability. To address this, we propose SwitchCoT, an automatic framework that adaptively chooses between long and short CoT strategies to balance reasoning accuracy and computational efficiency. Moreover, SwitchCoT is designed to be budget-aware, making it broadly applicable across scenarios with varying resource constraints. Experimental results demonstrate that SwitchCoT can reduce inference costs by up to 50% while maintaining high accuracy. Notably, under limited token budgets, it achieves performance comparable to, or even exceeding, that of using either long or short CoT alone.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Long or short CoT? Investigating Instance-level Switch of Large Reasoning Models":

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the trade-offs between long Chain-of-Thought (CoT) and short CoT prompting strategies for large reasoning models (LRMs). Through empirical analysis, it finds that long CoT, while generally improving accuracy, often leads to only marginal gains relative to its significantly higher token consumption. It highlights that short CoT can be more effective under tighter budget constraints. Based on these findings, the paper proposes SwitchCoT, a budget-aware framework that dynamically switches between long and short CoT strategies at the instance level to balance reasoning accuracy and computational efficiency. Experiments show that SwitchCoT can reduce token usage by up to 50% while maintaining or even improving accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the development of SwitchCoT, a practical framework that adaptively chooses between long and short CoT based on task context and resource constraints. While the idea of using different CoT lengths isn't entirely new, SwitchCoT's automatic, instance-level adaptation and budget-awareness differentiate it from previous work. The empirical analysis comparing long and short CoT provides valuable insights into their respective strengths and weaknesses. However, the adaptive framework may be seen as a straightforward application of existing methods for selecting prompts.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant practical implications for deploying and using LRMs in resource-constrained environments. Reducing token consumption is a major concern for many applications, and SwitchCoT offers a promising solution without sacrificing accuracy. The empirical findings also contribute to a better understanding of how CoT strategies affect performance and efficiency.
However, the novelty of the method is limited to application of already existing approaches and that may limit the impact of the paper.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Comprehensive empirical analysis of long vs. short CoT.
    *   A practical and budget-aware framework for adaptive CoT strategy selection.
    *   Demonstrated effectiveness in reducing token usage while maintaining accuracy.
    *   Extensive experiments across diverse tasks and models.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The SwitchCoT framework is straightforward and may not be considered deeply innovative.
    *   The strategy selector model, while effective, might benefit from more sophisticated design.
    *   The binary (long/short) formulation of CoT could be limiting; exploring more granular levels of deliberation could further improve the approach.
    *   There is no comparison with other CoT compression techniques in order to fully emphasize advantages of SwitchCoT.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on efficient reasoning and adaptive prompting for LRMs. It highlights the importance of considering resource constraints and task-specific characteristics when choosing CoT strategies. The SwitchCoT framework provides a concrete starting point for developing more sophisticated adaptive reasoning approaches.

**Justification for the Score:**

While the paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of efficient reasoning for LRMs, its novelty is moderate. The strength of the work lies in its thorough empirical analysis and its practical framework, SwitchCoT, which demonstrates significant gains in efficiency without sacrificing accuracy. The empirical analysis provides valuable insights and may open an avenue for future research.
However, the technique has limited novelty and has not been compared to alternative CoT compression techniques.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### R-Search: Empowering LLM Reasoning with Search via Multi-Reward Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04185v1)
- **Authors**: Qingfei Zhao, Ruobing Wang, Dingling Xu, Daren Zha, Limin Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have notably progressed in multi-step and long-chain reasoning. However, extending their reasoning capabilities to encompass deep interactions with search remains a non-trivial challenge, as models often fail to identify optimal reasoning-search interaction trajectories, resulting in suboptimal responses. We propose R-Search, a novel reinforcement learning framework for Reasoning-Search integration, designed to enable LLMs to autonomously execute multi-step reasoning with deep search interaction, and learn optimal reasoning search interaction trajectories via multi-reward signals, improving response quality in complex logic- and knowledge-intensive tasks. R-Search guides the LLM to dynamically decide when to retrieve or reason, while globally integrating key evidence to enhance deep knowledge interaction between reasoning and search. During RL training, R-Search provides multi-stage, multi-type rewards to jointly optimize the reasoning-search trajectory. Experiments on seven datasets show that R-Search outperforms advanced RAG baselines by up to 32.2% (in-domain) and 25.1% (out-of-domain). The code and data are available at https://github.com/QingFei1/R-Search.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R-Search, a reinforcement learning (RL) framework designed to enhance reasoning capabilities in Large Language Models (LLMs) by enabling dynamic interaction with external knowledge via search.  R-Search aims to improve the process of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) by allowing the LLM to decide *when* and *what* to retrieve at any point during the reasoning process, fostering a deeper integration of external knowledge.  The framework utilizes a multi-stage, multi-type reward mechanism that optimizes the reasoning-search trajectory using signals related to answer quality, evidence quality, and format correctness.  Experiments across various datasets demonstrate that R-Search outperforms several RAG baselines. The authors also present R-Search-as-a-Tool (RSTool), which modularizes the evidence component to be used for downstream tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using RL to optimize the *interaction* between reasoning and search in LLMs is reasonably novel. Traditional RAG approaches often use pre-defined or prompt-engineered strategies for retrieval. R-Search's dynamic token-level decision making for retrieval and the global integration of evidence are valuable contributions. The multi-stage reward mechanism and the RSTool are also well-thought-out contributions. There are concurrent works exploring RL for reasoning and search, but R-Search appears to offer specific advantages in reward design and deep knowledge integration.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in addressing a critical limitation of existing LLMs: their reliance on parametric knowledge and the challenges of effectively incorporating external knowledge. By learning an optimal reasoning-search strategy, R-Search improves response quality, especially on complex logic- and knowledge-intensive tasks. This has implications for improving the reliability and accuracy of LLMs in real-world applications. The RSTool component is a valuable modularity approach. The performance gains reported against competitive baselines provide empirical support for the effectiveness of the framework.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Dynamic Interaction:**  The framework's ability to dynamically interleave reasoning and search at a token level is a significant strength, allowing for more adaptive knowledge integration.
    *   **Multi-Reward Modeling:** The design of a comprehensive reward mechanism considers answer quality, evidence quality, and format correctness, promoting robust policy learning.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The thorough experimental evaluation on multiple datasets and comparison with strong baselines provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of R-Search.
    *   **R-Search-as-a-Tool (RSTool):** Provides a means of sharing learned evidence.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **RL Complexity:** RL training can be computationally expensive and sensitive to hyperparameter tuning. The paper provides implementation details, but further analysis of the training stability and sensitivity would strengthen the work.
    *   **Dataset Limitation:** The use of only 2WikiMQA for training (acknowledged in the limitations section) might restrict generalization. While out-of-domain performance is reported, training on a broader range of datasets would enhance robustness.
    *   **Limited backbone choice:** The reliance primarily on Qwen models.
    *   **Explainability:** While R-Search improves performance, the paper could delve deeper into *why* certain reasoning-search trajectories are preferred. Understanding the learned retrieval and reasoning strategies would be valuable.

*   **Potential Influence:** R-Search has the potential to influence the direction of research in RAG and LLM reasoning.  The framework's approach to dynamic interaction and multi-reward optimization could inspire new methods for knowledge integration and policy learning. The R-Search-as-a-Tool provides a means for additional research opportunities.

**Justification for Score:**

While the use of RL for RAG is not entirely new, the specific implementation of R-Search, with its dynamic token-level retrieval, multi-stage reward modeling, and empirical validation, constitutes a notable contribution. The paper addresses a relevant and challenging problem in LLM research. However, the dataset limitations in training and the relative complexity of RL training prevent a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### TracLLM: A Generic Framework for Attributing Long Context LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04202v1)
- **Authors**: Yanting Wang, Wei Zou, Runpeng Geng, Jinyuan Jia
- **Abstract**: Long context large language models (LLMs) are deployed in many real-world applications such as RAG, agent, and broad LLM-integrated applications. Given an instruction and a long context (e.g., documents, PDF files, webpages), a long context LLM can generate an output grounded in the provided context, aiming to provide more accurate, up-to-date, and verifiable outputs while reducing hallucinations and unsupported claims. This raises a research question: how to pinpoint the texts (e.g., sentences, passages, or paragraphs) in the context that contribute most to or are responsible for the generated output by an LLM? This process, which we call context traceback, has various real-world applications, such as 1) debugging LLM-based systems, 2) conducting post-attack forensic analysis for attacks (e.g., prompt injection attack, knowledge corruption attacks) to an LLM, and 3) highlighting knowledge sources to enhance the trust of users towards outputs generated by LLMs. When applied to context traceback for long context LLMs, existing feature attribution methods such as Shapley have sub-optimal performance and/or incur a large computational cost. In this work, we develop TracLLM, the first generic context traceback framework tailored to long context LLMs. Our framework can improve the effectiveness and efficiency of existing feature attribution methods. To improve the efficiency, we develop an informed search based algorithm in TracLLM. We also develop contribution score ensemble/denoising techniques to improve the accuracy of TracLLM. Our evaluation results show TracLLM can effectively identify texts in a long context that lead to the output of an LLM. Our code and data are at: https://github.com/Wang-Yanting/TracLLM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TracLLM: A Generic Framework for Attributing Long Context LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TracLLM, a novel framework for tracing the origins of an LLM's output back to specific texts within its input context. The framework is designed for long-context LLMs and addresses the challenge of efficiently and accurately pinpointing the most influential segments of a long document that contribute to the generated output.  TracLLM employs an informed search algorithm to reduce the computational cost associated with existing feature attribution methods like Shapley values, and incorporates techniques such as contribution score denoising and ensemble methods to improve accuracy.  The paper demonstrates TracLLM's effectiveness across various real-world applications, including debugging LLM systems, post-attack forensic analysis (identifying malicious injected texts), and enhancing user trust by tracing knowledge sources.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the development of a practical and efficient framework for context traceback in long-context LLMs. While feature attribution methods exist, their application to long contexts becomes computationally prohibitive. TracLLM addresses this by combining an informed search strategy with denoising and ensemble techniques, leading to improved efficiency and accuracy. This is a significant advancement given the growing prevalence of long-context models. The idea of informed search tailored to context traceback appears to be a key contribution.

*   **Significance:** The applications of TracLLM are of considerable significance. The ability to debug LLM-based systems, conduct post-attack forensic analysis (e.g., identifying injected malicious code), and trace knowledge sources has far-reaching implications for the trustworthiness and reliability of LLMs. This is especially important as LLMs are deployed in critical domains such as information retrieval, decision support, and autonomous agents. The forensic analysis use case is particularly compelling in a world where LLM security is paramount. The ability to trace back harmful outputs to their source materials can help mitigate risks associated with prompt injection, data poisoning, and other adversarial attacks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practicality:** The framework is designed to be generic and compatible with existing feature attribution methods, making it easily adaptable and deployable.
    *   **Efficiency:** The informed search algorithm provides a substantial improvement in computational efficiency compared to directly applying methods like Shapley values to long contexts.
    *   **Improved Accuracy:** Contribution score denoising and ensemble techniques enhance the accuracy of context traceback.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation on a variety of benchmark datasets, applications, and LLMs.
    *   **Theoretical Analysis:** The inclusion of a theoretical analysis lends further support to the effectiveness of TracLLM.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** While more efficient than direct application of Shapley, TracLLM still has non-negligible computational cost, which might limit its applicability in latency-sensitive applications. This is a clear limitation, though the authors acknowledge it and suggest it as future work.
    *   **Reliance on Existing Methods:** TracLLM relies on existing feature attribution methods. Therefore, its performance is ultimately bounded by the limitations of the underlying method.
    *   **Limited Discussion on Scalability:** The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion on the scalability of TracLLM with extremely long contexts (e.g., millions of tokens) and on how the framework might be optimized for such scenarios.
    *   **Black-box LLMs**: The techniques for calculating conditional probability may not be possible for black box LLMs without API access.

*   **Potential Influence:** TracLLM has the potential to influence research in several areas, including:

    *   Explainable AI (XAI) for LLMs
    *   LLM security and adversarial robustness
    *   Debugging and testing of LLM-based systems
    *   Knowledge management and source attribution

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper clearly articulates the problem, proposes a well-engineered solution, and provides convincing empirical evidence to support its claims. The limitations are appropriately acknowledged.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

I assign a score of 8 because TracLLM offers a significant and practical contribution to the field by providing a generic and efficient framework for context traceback in long-context LLMs. It addresses a key challenge associated with applying feature attribution methods to long contexts and offers a compelling set of applications, particularly in the area of LLM security. While further optimizations for extreme-scale contexts are needed, the framework is well-designed and thoroughly evaluated.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Cascadia: A Cascade Serving System for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04203v1)
- **Authors**: Youhe Jiang, Fangcheng Fu, Wanru Zhao, Stephan Rabanser, Nicholas D. Lane, Binhang Yuan
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have intensified the need to deliver both rapid responses and high-quality answers. More powerful models yield better results but incur higher inference latency, whereas smaller models are faster yet less capable. Recent work proposes balancing this latency-quality trade-off using model cascades, which route simpler queries to smaller models and more complex ones to larger models. However, enabling efficient cascade serving remains challenging. Current frameworks lack effective mechanisms for handling (i) the huge and varying resource demands of different LLMs, (ii) the inherent heterogeneity of LLM workloads, and (iii) the co-optimization of system deployment and routing strategy. Motivated by these observations, we introduce Cascadia, a novel cascade serving framework designed explicitly to schedule request routing and deploy model cascades for fast, quality-preserving LLM serving. Cascadia employs a bi-level optimization method: at the inner level, it uses a mixed-integer linear program to select resource allocations and parallelism strategies based on LLM information and workload characteristics; at the outer level, it applies a weighted Tchebycheff algorithm to iteratively co-optimize the routing strategy and the system deployment produced by the inner level. Our extensive evaluation on diverse workload traces and different model cascades (DeepSeek and the Llama series) demonstrates that Cascadia significantly outperforms both single-model deployments and the state-of-the-art cascade serving baseline, achieving up to 4x (2.3x on average) tighter latency SLOs and up to 5x (2.4x on average) higher throughput while maintaining target answer quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the CASCADIA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CASCADIA, a novel cascade serving system for large language models (LLMs).  The core problem it tackles is the latency-quality trade-off in serving LLMs, where larger models offer better quality but slower response times. CASCADIA uses a model cascade architecture, routing simpler queries to smaller, faster models and more complex queries to larger, more capable models.  The key innovation lies in its scheduling algorithm, which employs a bi-level optimization approach. The inner level uses Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP) to determine optimal resource allocation and parallelism strategies, given a specific routing strategy. The outer level uses a weighted Tchebycheff method to co-optimize the routing strategy and system deployment based on latency and quality.  Experimental results demonstrate that CASCADIA significantly outperforms single-model deployments and existing cascade serving systems in terms of latency SLO attainment and throughput.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a solid, but not ground-breaking, contribution to the field of LLM serving.  Model cascades are not entirely new, and prior work has explored optimizing resource allocation in LLM serving. However, the paper’s novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques and the comprehensive co-optimization approach. The use of a bi-level optimization framework with MILP for resource allocation and a weighted Tchebycheff method for routing is a significant improvement over existing systems. Existing works don't fully consider LLM-specific workload characteristics and don't completely co-optimize deployment with request routing.  CASCADIA addresses the need for LLM-specific optimization that better handles varying resource requirements, workload heterogeneity, and model deployment optimization.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a highly relevant and practical problem.  Efficiently serving LLMs is crucial for their widespread adoption, and improving both latency and throughput is essential.  The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of CASCADIA, showing substantial gains over existing methods across various workload traces and quality requirements. The demonstrated ability to significantly tighten latency SLOs and increase throughput while maintaining quality is a valuable contribution that can have a real impact. However, the cluster size is on the lower end of deployment configurations, which could benefit from being more robust.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Co-optimization:** The bi-level optimization approach effectively co-optimizes resource allocation, parallelism, and routing strategies, leading to significant performance improvements.
    * **LLM-Specific Focus:** CASCADIA is designed explicitly for LLMs, taking into account their unique resource needs and workload characteristics.
    * **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper provides extensive experimental results that demonstrate the superiority of CASCADIA over existing methods.
    * **Clear Problem Formulation and Solution:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and presents a well-defined solution.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The MILP-based inner optimization and the weighted Tchebycheff outer optimization add significant complexity to the system. While the experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness, the complexity may make it more challenging to implement and deploy in practice. Future work could discuss the overall system performance by evaluating a single, consolidated metric.
    * **Limited Scalability Evaluation:** All experiments were conducted on a 32-GPU cluster.  While representative, evaluating the system's performance on larger GPU pools would further validate its scalability.
    * **Homogeneous Cluster Assumption:** The framework assumes a homogeneous cluster, which may not be realistic in all deployment scenarios. Addressing heterogeneous clusters would broaden the applicability of CASCADIA.
    * **Simulation Dependency:** The inner optimization relies on a simulator to estimate the latency.  The accuracy of the simulation is critical, and the reliance on it could be a limitation. A deeper investigation into the simulator's accuracy and its impact on the overall system performance would be helpful.

* **Potential Influence:**  CASCADIA is likely to influence future research in LLM serving.  The paper’s focus on co-optimization and LLM-specific characteristics will likely inspire other researchers to explore similar approaches. The paper also provides a valuable benchmark for comparing different LLM serving systems.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **7**. While the core ideas are not entirely novel, the comprehensive co-optimization approach and the LLM-specific focus represent a significant advancement. The experimental results are convincing, and the paper addresses a practical and important problem. However, the complexity of the system, the limited scalability evaluation, the homogeneous cluster assumption, and the reliance on simulation are notable weaknesses that prevent a higher score. It builds off prior work effectively, contributing to a valuable understanding of LLM optimization.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### EPiC: Towards Lossless Speedup for Reasoning Training through Edge-Preserving CoT Condensation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04205v1)
- **Authors**: Jinghan Jia, Hadi Reisizadeh, Chongyu Fan, Nathalie Baracaldo, Mingyi Hong, Sijia Liu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown remarkable reasoning capabilities when trained with chain-of-thought (CoT) supervision. However, the long and verbose CoT traces, especially those distilled from large reasoning models (LRMs) such as DeepSeek-R1, significantly increase training costs during the distillation process, where a non-reasoning base model is taught to replicate the reasoning behavior of an LRM. In this work, we study the problem of CoT condensation for resource-efficient reasoning training, aimed at pruning intermediate reasoning steps (i.e., thoughts) in CoT traces, enabling supervised model training on length-reduced CoT data while preserving both answer accuracy and the model's ability to generate coherent reasoning. Our rationale is that CoT traces typically follow a three-stage structure: problem understanding, exploration, and solution convergence. Through empirical analysis, we find that retaining the structure of the reasoning trace, especially the early stage of problem understanding (rich in reflective cues) and the final stage of solution convergence, is sufficient to achieve lossless reasoning supervision. To this end, we propose an Edge-Preserving Condensation method, EPiC, which selectively retains only the initial and final segments of each CoT trace while discarding the middle portion. This design draws an analogy to preserving the "edge" of a reasoning trajectory, capturing both the initial problem framing and the final answer synthesis, to maintain logical continuity. Experiments across multiple model families (Qwen and LLaMA) and benchmarks show that EPiC reduces training time by over 34% while achieving lossless reasoning accuracy on MATH500, comparable to full CoT supervision. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first study to explore thought-level CoT condensation for efficient reasoning model distillation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Edge-Preserving Condensation (EPiC), a method for efficient reasoning training of large language models (LLMs) with chain-of-thought (CoT) supervision. EPiC selectively retains the initial (problem understanding) and final (solution convergence) segments of CoT traces while discarding the intermediate reasoning steps. The authors argue that this "edge-preserving" approach maintains the structural integrity of reasoning, leading to competitive accuracy with significantly reduced training time. They provide empirical evidence across multiple model families and benchmarks demonstrating that EPiC achieves lossless reasoning accuracy while reducing training time by over 34%. The paper includes mutual information (MI) analysis and CoT perturbation studies to justify the EPiC design and demonstrate that the middle portion of CoT traces is less informative.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its approach to CoT condensation at the *thought level*, selectively pruning intermediate reasoning steps rather than tokens or entire examples. This is a departure from existing efficiency methods in CoT distillation, which primarily focus on reducing the number of training examples or compressing tokens. The identification of the three-stage structure of CoT traces (understanding, exploration, convergence) and the subsequent edge-preserving condensation is also a relatively novel contribution. Prior work explored example pruning and token pruning but this paper proposes an intermediary position, between tokens and examples, leading to a novel and effective method.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial bottleneck in reasoning training: the high computational cost of long and verbose CoT traces, especially those generated by large reasoning models (LRMs). By demonstrating that significant speedups can be achieved without sacrificing accuracy, the paper offers a practical and accessible approach for distilling reasoning capabilities into smaller LLMs.  This has the potential to democratize access to reasoning-enhanced LLMs, especially for resource-constrained researchers. The MI analysis and perturbation studies contribute to a better understanding of the information content within CoT traces, which can inform future research in this area.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the efficiency-accuracy trade-off in CoT-based reasoning training.
*   **Intuitive Method:** EPiC is conceptually simple and easy to implement.
*   **Strong Empirical Validation:** The paper presents extensive experimental results across diverse datasets, benchmarks, and model families, supporting the effectiveness of EPiC.
*   **Thorough Analysis:** The MI analysis and perturbation studies provide valuable insights into the reasoning process and justify the design choices of EPiC.
*   **Practical Implications:** EPiC offers a practical and efficient solution for reasoning training, enabling faster distillation of reasoning capabilities into smaller LLMs.
*   **Well-written and Organized:** The paper is well-structured and easy to follow.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Heuristic Segmentation:** The segmentation of CoT traces into understanding, exploration, and convergence stages is based on heuristics and may not always accurately reflect the underlying semantic structure of reasoning. A deeper analysis of these segments and a more sophisticated method for identifying them could further improve the method.
*   **Global Condensation Ratio:** The condensation ratio is applied uniformly across all examples, regardless of their difficulty.  Adaptive condensation strategies that adjust the pruning based on example complexity might lead to even better performance.
*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation primarily focuses on mathematical reasoning tasks. The generalizability of EPiC to other reasoning domains, such as legal or commonsense reasoning, requires further investigation.
*   **SFT only evaluation:** The evaluation is conducted only on SFT framework, and does not evaluate the method on RL-based finetuning, which could allow the model to dynamically prune traces during training.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper offers a practical and effective solution for efficient reasoning training, it's not without its limitations. The heuristic nature of the CoT trace segmentation and the globally applied condensation ratio are potential areas for improvement. Also, the method's generalizability to domains outside mathematics is not fully established. However, the thought-level pruning strategy and extensive empirical validation justify a relatively high score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Advancing Multimodal Reasoning: From Optimized Cold Start to Staged Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04207v1)
- **Authors**: Shuang Chen, Yue Guo, Zhaochen Su, Yafu Li, Yulun Wu, Jiacheng Chen, Jiayu Chen, Weijie Wang, Xiaoye Qu, Yu Cheng
- **Abstract**: Inspired by the remarkable reasoning capabilities of Deepseek-R1 in complex textual tasks, many works attempt to incentivize similar capabilities in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) by directly applying reinforcement learning (RL). However, they still struggle to activate complex reasoning. In this paper, rather than examining multimodal RL in isolation, we delve into current training pipelines and identify three crucial phenomena: 1) Effective cold start initialization is critical for enhancing MLLM reasoning. Intriguingly, we find that initializing with carefully selected text data alone can lead to performance surpassing many recent multimodal reasoning models, even before multimodal RL. 2) Standard GRPO applied to multimodal RL suffers from gradient stagnation, which degrades training stability and performance. 3) Subsequent text-only RL training, following the multimodal RL phase, further enhances multimodal reasoning. This staged training approach effectively balances perceptual grounding and cognitive reasoning development. By incorporating the above insights and addressing multimodal RL issues, we introduce ReVisual-R1, achieving a new state-of-the-art among open-source 7B MLLMs on challenging benchmarks including MathVerse, MathVision, WeMath, LogicVista, DynaMath, and challenging AIME2024 and AIME2025.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the research paper:

**Summary**

The paper "Advancing Multimodal Reasoning: From Optimized Cold Start to Staged Reinforcement Learning" introduces ReVisual-R1, a 7B parameter open-source multimodal large language model (MLLM) designed to improve complex reasoning capabilities. The key innovation is a three-stage training curriculum consisting of: 1) a carefully curated text-based cold start to establish strong foundational language understanding and reasoning skills; 2) multimodal reinforcement learning (MRL) using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) enhanced with Prioritized Advantage Distillation (PAD) to address gradient stagnation issues and promote more stable and efficient learning; and 3) a text-only reinforcement learning (TRL) phase to refine linguistic fluency and advanced reasoning.  The authors demonstrate that this staged approach, along with the PAD algorithm, leads to state-of-the-art performance among open-source 7B MLLMs on challenging benchmarks, even surpassing some closed-source commercial models in specific areas. The paper emphasizes the importance of data curation and algorithmic optimization over sheer model scale.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty in Training Paradigm:** The paper presents a well-reasoned and empirically supported staged training curriculum. The core concept of first establishing strong text-based reasoning followed by multimodal grounding and subsequent textual refinement seems sound and addresses a key bottleneck in current MLLM training.
    *   **Addressing Gradient Stagnation:**  The Prioritized Advantage Distillation (PAD) algorithm is a significant contribution. Identifying and addressing the gradient stagnation issue in multimodal RL using GRPO is a concrete and valuable finding. The proposed solution (PAD) appears technically sound and is backed by ablation studies.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper showcases extensive experimental results on a wide range of challenging benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of ReVisual-R1 and the proposed training approach. The comparisons against both open-source and closed-source models add credibility to the claims.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:** Releasing ReVisual-R1 as an open-source model is a significant contribution to the community, enabling further research and development in the field. The reproducibility is enhanced by the provision of the code page and detailed implementation descriptions.
    *   **Emphasis on Data Curation:** The effort to create the GRAMMAR dataset underscores the importance of high-quality, reasoning-focused training data.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Theoretical Justification for Staging:** While the empirical results are strong, the theoretical underpinnings explaining *why* this specific three-stage curriculum is optimal could be more developed. A deeper analysis of the interplay between perceptual grounding and cognitive reasoning in MLLMs would strengthen the argument.
    *   **Dependency on Specific Architectures:** The model is built on Qwen2.5-VL-7B. It would be beneficial to show how the proposed training strategy generalizes to other MLLM architectures to prove that it is not architecture specific.
    *   **Scope of Generalization:** Despite strong performance on reasoning tasks, the paper could benefit from a more thorough investigation of ReVisual-R1's performance on other downstream tasks (e.g., image captioning, visual question answering beyond math/logic), to better understand its generalizability.
    *   **Ablation Study Completeness:** While PAD components are ablated, more detailed parameter sensitivity analysis of Thigh, Tlow, and p within the PAD framework could improve its adoption and application in other MLLM training scenarios.
    *   **Justification of Efficient-Length Reward:** While the concept is intuitive, the justification of the chosen values for parameters such as *a* and delta would strengthen the reward design.

*   **Significance and Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper's findings have the potential to significantly influence the MLLM training landscape. The emphasis on optimized cold starts and staged reinforcement learning offers a practical and effective approach for improving reasoning capabilities.
    *   The PAD algorithm has the potential to be widely adopted in MLLM training, as it directly addresses a known issue (gradient stagnation) and offers a computationally efficient solution.
    *   The open-source release of ReVisual-R1 can serve as a valuable benchmark for future research and development in the field.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper addresses a critical challenge in the development of MLLMs: achieving robust reasoning capabilities. The proposed three-stage training curriculum, especially the PAD algorithm, offers a concrete and well-validated solution. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of ReVisual-R1 over existing open-source alternatives. The significance of this paper lies in its emphasis on principled training strategies and algorithmic enhancements, rather than simply scaling model size. The open-source release of ReVisual-R1 will further amplify its impact on the research community. While there are areas for improvement, as mentioned above, the novelty and potential influence of this work are substantial.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Diffusion Domain Teacher: Diffusion Guided Domain Adaptive Object Detector
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04211v1)
- **Authors**: Boyong He, Yuxiang Ji, Zhuoyue Tan, Liaoni Wu
- **Abstract**: Object detectors often suffer a decrease in performance due to the large domain gap between the training data (source domain) and real-world data (target domain). Diffusion-based generative models have shown remarkable abilities in generating high-quality and diverse images, suggesting their potential for extracting valuable feature from various domains. To effectively leverage the cross-domain feature representation of diffusion models, in this paper, we train a detector with frozen-weight diffusion model on the source domain, then employ it as a teacher model to generate pseudo labels on the unlabeled target domain, which are used to guide the supervised learning of the student model on the target domain. We refer to this approach as Diffusion Domain Teacher (DDT). By employing this straightforward yet potent framework, we significantly improve cross-domain object detection performance without compromising the inference speed. Our method achieves an average mAP improvement of 21.2% compared to the baseline on 6 datasets from three common cross-domain detection benchmarks (Cross-Camera, Syn2Real, Real2Artistic}, surpassing the current state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods by an average of 5.7% mAP. Furthermore, extensive experiments demonstrate that our method consistently brings improvements even in more powerful and complex models, highlighting broadly applicable and effective domain adaptation capability of our DDT. The code is available at https://github.com/heboyong/Diffusion-Domain-Teacher.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Diffusion Domain Teacher" (DDT), a novel unsupervised domain adaptation (UDA) method for object detection. DDT leverages diffusion models as feature extractors to bridge the domain gap between source (labeled) and target (unlabeled) datasets. The core idea is to use a frozen-weight diffusion model, trained on the source domain, as a teacher model to generate pseudo-labels for the target domain.  These pseudo-labels then guide the training of a student detector on the target domain, with a mean teacher framework employed for stability and generalization.  The authors demonstrate significant improvements in cross-domain object detection performance across several benchmark datasets, surpassing existing state-of-the-art methods without increasing inference time. They show the diffusion model captures generalized features, allowing the teacher to generate high-quality pseudo-labels.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The use of diffusion models for UDA in object detection is relatively novel. While diffusion models are being explored in various tasks, their direct application as feature extractors within a self-training framework for domain adaptation, particularly with the focus on retaining inference speed, is a noteworthy contribution.
*   **Significance:** The problem of domain shift remains a major hurdle in deploying object detectors in real-world scenarios. DDT directly addresses this problem and shows considerable performance gains without significantly impacting computational cost. This makes the approach practical and potentially impactful for real-world applications.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents extensive experimental results across several challenging datasets, consistently outperforming existing SOTA methods.  The mAP improvements of 21.2% are substantial.
    *   **Elegant and Simple Framework:** DDT's simplicity is a strength. The framework is relatively straightforward to implement and doesn't rely on complex adversarial training or intricate loss functions.
    *   **Maintains Inference Speed:** The use of a frozen-weight diffusion model backbone means there's no increase to inference time, a critical consideration for real-time object detection systems.
    *   **Versatility:** The method is shown to improve cross-domain performance even for detectors using complex backbone architectures like ConvNext.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:**  The paper focuses primarily on empirical results and lacks a deeper theoretical analysis of *why* the diffusion model captures domain-invariant features. While the paper suggests an explanation, more rigorous justification would strengthen the work.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Diffusion Model Parameters:** The paper uses a specific pre-trained diffusion model architecture. A more thorough investigation into how different diffusion model parameters (e.g., number of steps, noise schedule) impact performance would be beneficial.
    *   **Dependence on pre-trained diffusion model:** A completely self trained diffusion based model (without using pretrained weight) would make a significant difference to the significance and usage scope of this project
    *   **Foggy Cityscapes results could be better:** While good in general, this specific dataset does not show similar benefits as other datasets. An explanation or further analysis to support this special scenario would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in UDA for object detection.  The use of generative models for feature extraction, the focus on practical speed considerations, and the consistent performance gains are likely to encourage further exploration in this direction.
*   **Justification of score:** While the paper demonstrates a novel approach with strong empirical backing and practical benefits, the limited theoretical analysis and the areas that could be explored more deeply reduce the potential impact.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### FullDiT2: Efficient In-Context Conditioning for Video Diffusion Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04213v1)
- **Authors**: Xuanhua He, Quande Liu, Zixuan Ye, Wecai Ye, Qiulin Wang, Xintao Wang, Qifeng Chen, Pengfei Wan, Di Zhang, Kun Gai
- **Abstract**: Fine-grained and efficient controllability on video diffusion transformers has raised increasing desires for the applicability. Recently, In-context Conditioning emerged as a powerful paradigm for unified conditional video generation, which enables diverse controls by concatenating varying context conditioning signals with noisy video latents into a long unified token sequence and jointly processing them via full-attention, e.g., FullDiT. Despite their effectiveness, these methods face quadratic computation overhead as task complexity increases, hindering practical deployment. In this paper, we study the efficiency bottleneck neglected in original in-context conditioning video generation framework. We begin with systematic analysis to identify two key sources of the computation inefficiencies: the inherent redundancy within context condition tokens and the computational redundancy in context-latent interactions throughout the diffusion process. Based on these insights, we propose FullDiT2, an efficient in-context conditioning framework for general controllability in both video generation and editing tasks, which innovates from two key perspectives. Firstly, to address the token redundancy, FullDiT2 leverages a dynamic token selection mechanism to adaptively identify important context tokens, reducing the sequence length for unified full-attention. Additionally, a selective context caching mechanism is devised to minimize redundant interactions between condition tokens and video latents. Extensive experiments on six diverse conditional video editing and generation tasks demonstrate that FullDiT2 achieves significant computation reduction and 2-3 times speedup in averaged time cost per diffusion step, with minimal degradation or even higher performance in video generation quality. The project page is at \href{https://fulldit2.github.io/}{https://fulldit2.github.io/}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the FullDiT2 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "FullDiT2: Efficient In-Context Conditioning for Video Diffusion Transformers" addresses the computational inefficiencies in In-Context Conditioning (ICC) for video generation and editing using diffusion transformers (DiTs). The original in-context conditioning method, exemplified by FullDiT, suffers from quadratic computation overhead due to increased sequence lengths caused by concatenating context condition tokens with noisy video latents. FullDiT2 tackles this issue by introducing two key innovations: (1) a dynamic token selection mechanism that adaptively identifies and selects important context tokens, reducing the sequence length for full-attention, and (2) a selective context caching mechanism that minimizes redundant interactions between condition tokens and video latents throughout the diffusion process by caching and skipping.  Experiments across various video editing and generation tasks demonstrate significant computation reduction and speedups with minimal performance degradation, or even performance improvements in video quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers moderate novelty. The core idea of addressing computational bottlenecks in ICC is relevant, and the proposed solutions – dynamic token selection and selective context caching – are intuitive and logical. Dynamic token selection is not entirely novel as attention based token selection and filtering are established research avenues. The novel aspect comes from the caching and skip mechanism, but caching and reusing intermediate features have seen earlier studies in diffuison transformers. It would have benefited the paper by providing a comparison of the proposed token selection strategies versus alternative token selection based on the attention weights directly. While there's prior work on reducing computation in DiTs, specifically targetting the noisy video latents pathway, FullDiT2 is specifically designed to address inefficiencies arising from reference condition tokens within the unified ICC sequence, that distinguishes from existing acceleration work, but not in a radically novel way. The core idea of caching and skipping has been studied with cached diffusion features to improve inference speeds. The novelty lies in a unified, well-engineered system.

*   **Significance:** The significance is reasonably high. Controllable video generation and editing is an active research area, and the paper's focus on efficiency directly impacts the practical applicability of DiTs. By significantly reducing the computational burden, FullDiT2 makes it easier to deploy these models in real-world scenarios, especially as tasks become more complex. The performance improvements, or at least the preservation of performance while drastically improving speed, are a valuable contribution to the community. The extensive experimentation across diverse tasks strengthens the paper's claims and demonstrates generalizability. A weakness is that some of the gains will be specific to the base FullDiT model. It could have benefitted from comparing to alternative efficient DiT architectures.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated solutions.
    *   Thorough analysis of the computational redundancy within ICC.
    *   Effective integration of dynamic token selection and selective context caching.
    *   Extensive experimental validation across six diverse video conditioning tasks.
    *   Demonstrated practical improvements in speed and resource usage.
    * Decoupled Attention is analyzed from an equivalent masked attention.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    * Limited "wow" factor: the solutions are incremental improvements rather than groundbreaking conceptual leaps.
    *   Lack of ablation study on the selection strategies themselves, which leaves room for future optimization.
    * Limited generalization of the efficiency gains given a base FullDiT model.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper will likely be well-received by the video generation and editing research community. It provides practical insights and a concrete implementation that can be readily adopted and extended. The ideas of adaptive token selection and selective caching are likely to influence future research directions in this area.

**Justification:**

The score reflects the solid engineering and practical value of the work, but also acknowledges the lack of major conceptual breakthroughs. The paper's strength lies in its practical focus and demonstrated improvements in efficiency without sacrificing performance. The impact will likely be felt in the adoption of FullDiT2's techniques by other researchers working on controllable video generation. While not revolutionary, the paper offers a significant step forward in making these techniques more accessible and practical.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sounding that Object: Interactive Object-Aware Image to Audio Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.04214v1)
- **Authors**: Tingle Li, Baihe Huang, Xiaobin Zhuang, Dongya Jia, Jiawei Chen, Yuping Wang, Zhuo Chen, Gopala Anumanchipalli, Yuxuan Wang
- **Abstract**: Generating accurate sounds for complex audio-visual scenes is challenging, especially in the presence of multiple objects and sound sources. In this paper, we propose an {\em interactive object-aware audio generation} model that grounds sound generation in user-selected visual objects within images. Our method integrates object-centric learning into a conditional latent diffusion model, which learns to associate image regions with their corresponding sounds through multi-modal attention. At test time, our model employs image segmentation to allow users to interactively generate sounds at the {\em object} level. We theoretically validate that our attention mechanism functionally approximates test-time segmentation masks, ensuring the generated audio aligns with selected objects. Quantitative and qualitative evaluations show that our model outperforms baselines, achieving better alignment between objects and their associated sounds. Project page: https://tinglok.netlify.app/files/avobject/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an interactive object-aware audio generation model that allows users to generate sounds based on user-selected objects within an image. The method uses a conditional latent diffusion model enhanced with multi-modal attention to learn associations between image regions and corresponding sounds. At test time, users interact with the model by selecting objects via segmentation masks, which are then used to guide the audio generation process. The authors provide theoretical validation showing the attention mechanism approximates segmentation masks, and empirical results demonstrate the model outperforms baselines in sound-object alignment and user controllability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The approach is novel in its interactive, object-aware approach to audio generation.  It moves beyond generating holistic soundscapes or relying solely on text prompts by allowing users to directly influence the audio generation based on visual objects. The use of pre-trained models (CLIP, SAM, etc.) and fine-tuning them to interactively generate object-aware audio is a reasonable novel idea.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The method is technically sound, integrating a latent diffusion model with multi-modal attention and user-provided segmentation masks. The theoretical analysis provides a valuable justification for the attention mechanism's behavior. It presents a solid theoretical grounding for the connection between the attention mechanism and segmentation.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents comprehensive quantitative and qualitative results, comparing the model against several strong baselines. The inclusion of human perceptual studies further strengthens the empirical validation. The paper demonstrates that the object-aware method yields higher ratings in perceptual quality and relevance to specific objects.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Pre-trained Models:** The model relies heavily on pre-trained models such as CLIP and SAM. While this is a common practice, it limits the novelty and potential for significant breakthroughs in the core audio generation or object understanding components. This reduces the scope for more foundational contributions.
    *   **Limited Scope of "Interactions":** While the model offers object-level control, the interactions are primarily limited to object selection via masks. Deeper forms of interaction, such as manipulating object attributes (e.g., material, action), are not explored.
    *   **Lack of Dynamic Scene Handling:**  The model processes static images and struggles with dynamic scenes or audio synchronized with non-stationary events. This limits its applicability to more realistic scenarios. This limitation is acknowledged, but its impact on real-world applicability is significant.
    *   **Incrementality:** While the model offers novel integrations, the incremental contributions in machine learning or deep learning are limited.
    *   **Dependency on High-Quality Segmentations:** The model relies on segmentation masks generated by SAM. Imperfect segmentation can affect the quality of the generated audio. There is limited exploration of how the model handles segmentation errors.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The interactive object-aware approach offers a promising direction for more controllable and targeted audio generation. It paves the way for applications in content creation, accessibility, and interactive experiences.
    *   The work demonstrates the potential of grounding audio generation in visual objects, moving beyond holistic scene analysis or text-based generation.
    *   The limitations point towards avenues for future research, such as incorporating dynamic information, exploring more expressive interactions, and improving the robustness to segmentation errors.

*   **Overall Assessment:**
    *   The paper demonstrates a functional and reasonably novel object-aware audio generation approach. The object-aware audio is a solid and practical idea that allows for a reasonable degree of controllability. The model provides high quality audio which could be useful in various applications and settings. The combination and theoretical connection between attention mechanisms and segmentation are novel. However, the dependency on pre-trained models, the static nature of the input, and the limited exploration of deeper interactions temper the overall significance. The innovation is largely in the *integration* of existing techniques rather than a fundamental advancement.

Score: 6

**Rationale:**

A score of 6 reflects a reasonable level of novelty and significance, primarily driven by the interactive object-aware approach and the functional demonstration of the model.  The limitations, particularly the reliance on pre-trained models and static images, prevent a higher score. It introduces an incremental advancement rather than a revolutionary breakthrough.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10
